\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{s}{\left(R_{m,S_{s},k-1}-R_{m,m,k-1}\right)}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\mathrm{Res}_{\varphi}f_{\varphi}\right\rVert}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{s}{\left(R_{m,S_{s},k-1}-R_{m,m,k-1}\right)}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}\sum_{\varphi}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{\varphi}\right\rVert}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{s}{\left(R_{m,S_{s},k-1}-R_{m,m,k-1}\right)}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}^{2}.

The last equality follows from orthogonality of the
f_{\varphi}
.

To take care of the cross terms, we observe that for any
X\in\mathcal{J}_{\varphi}
and
S\subseteq[n]
, we have
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,S,k}}\mathcal{J}_{\varphi^{\prime}}\right]}=0
for any
\varphi^{\prime}\neq\varphi
. Then by
Lemma

23
we have

\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi\neq\varphi^{\prime}}\left\langle f_{\varphi},\sum_{s=1}^{t}c_{s}R_{m,S_{s},k}f_{\varphi^{\prime}}\right\rangle=0.

(2)

This completes the proof.
∎

Proof of
Claim

41
.

Without loss of generality assume that
\varphi(k-1)=\varphi(k)
so we can regard
\mathrm{Res}_{\varphi}f_{\varphi}
as a real function on
\{\pm 1\}^{m(k-1)}
. Then

\displaystyle\left\langle f_{\varphi},f_{\varphi}\right\rangle=\sum_{X\in\{\pm 1\}^{mk}}f_{\varphi}(X)^{2}=\sum_{X\in\mathcal{J}_{\varphi}}{\left({f_{\varphi}(X)}\right)}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{X^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}^{m(k-1)}}\left(\mathrm{Res}_{\varphi}f(X^{\prime})\right)^{2}=\left\langle\mathrm{Res}_{\varphi}f_{\varphi},\mathrm{Res}_{\varphi}f_{\varphi}\right\rangle.

To prove the second statement, we compute

\displaystyle\left\langle f_{\varphi},R_{m,S,k}f_{\varphi}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{X\in\{\pm 1\}^{mk}}f_{\varphi}(X)\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{mk}}f_{\varphi}(Y)\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,S,k}}Y\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{X\in\mathcal{J}_{\varphi}}f_{\varphi}(X)\sum_{Y\in\mathcal{J}_{\varphi}}f_{\varphi}(Y)\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,S,k}}Y\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{X^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}^{m(k-1)}}\mathrm{Res}_{\varphi}f_{\varphi}(X^{\prime})\sum_{Y^{\prime}\in\{\pm 1\}^{m(k-1)}}\mathrm{Res}_{\varphi}f_{\varphi}(Y^{\prime})\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X^{\prime}\to_{R_{m,S,k-1}}Y^{\prime}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathrm{Res}_{\varphi}f_{\varphi},R_{m,S,k-1}\mathrm{Res}_{\varphi}f_{\varphi}\right\rangle.

The second-to-last equality follows because the corresponding
X,Y
are in
\mathcal{J}_{\varphi}
.
∎

We now prove
Theorem

25
, deferring proofs of the remaining needed auxiliary results to
Section

5.2
,
Section

5.3
, and
Section

5.4
.

Theorem 42

(
Theorem

25
restated)
.

Let
m\geq 100
and let
2\leq k\leq 2^{m/10}
. Given any
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
, we have

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}\leq{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m/4}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
k
. In the base case
k=1
and the result holds by the following argument when we write
f=f_{2}
. Now assume that the result holds for real functions on
\{\pm 1\}^{m(k-1)}
.

Let
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
. Write
f=f_{0}+f_{1}+f_{2}
where
f_{0}
is supported on
B_{=0}
,
f_{1}
is supported on
B_{=1}
, and
f_{2}
is supported on
B_{\geq 2}
. By
Lemma

50
applied with both
R_{m,m-1,k}
and
R_{m,m,k}
and
B_{=0}
and
\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\setminus B_{=0}=B_{=1}\cup B_{\geq 2}
, the other cross terms vanish, and we have

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f_{0},(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f_{0}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f_{1},(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f_{1}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\;+{\left|\left\langle f_{2},(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f_{2}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|2\left\langle f_{1},(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f_{2}\right\rangle\right|}

(Self-adjointness (
Fact

16
))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{(k-1)^{2}}{2^{m/4}}\right)}\left\langle f_{0},f_{0}\right\rangle+{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{m^{5}k}{2^{m/2-2}}\right)}\left\langle f_{1},f_{1}\right\rangle+{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m/2}}\right)}\left\langle f_{2},f_{2}\right\rangle+\frac{\sqrt{m}k}{2^{m/2-2}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{1}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert}_{2}

(
Lemma

40
+ induction,
Corollary

48
,
Proposition

57
,
Lemma

49
, in that order)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k^{2}-k}{2^{m/4}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle+\frac{m^{5}k}{2^{m/2-3}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{1}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k^{2}-k}{2^{m/4}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle+\frac{k}{2^{m/3}}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m/4}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle.\qed

5.2
f
Supported on
B_{=1}

We now use
Lemma

23
to bound
{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}\leq{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,m-1,k}f\right\rangle\right|}
when
f
is supported only on
B_{=1}
and the cross terms contributed. We first define a partition of
B_{=1}
, and apply
Lemma

23
to these different parts. One of our main observations to bound
f
supported on
B_{=1}
is the observation that when
k
is small, it is highly unlikely that two vectors out of any
k
are close to each other. Thus, a random walk beginning in
B_{=1}
and obeying the transition probabilities given by
B_{m,m-1,k}
will rarely remain in
B_{=1}
. Formally,
Lemma

23
bounds the contributions to the spectral norm by these transition probabilities.

Definition 43
.

For
S\subseteq[m]
define the set
\mathcal{I}_{S}\subseteq\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
by

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{S}={\left\{X\in B_{=1}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\forall a\in S,\exists i,j\in[k]\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\Delta{\left(X^{i},X^{j}\right)}=\{a\}\right\}}.

Unfortunately, the sets
\mathcal{I}_{S}
for different
S\subseteq[m]
do not form a partition of
B_{=1}
, since there is overlap between
\mathcal{I}_{S}
and
\mathcal{I}_{T}
for
S\neq T
. However, we can artificially make this into a partition.

Definition 44
.

For
S\subseteq[m]
with
|S|=1
(so
S=\{a\}
for some
a\in[m]
) define the set
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}\subseteq\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
by

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}=\mathcal{I}_{S}\setminus\bigcup_{a^{\prime}\neq a}\mathcal{I}_{\{a^{\prime}\}}.

Now place an arbitrary ordering
\preceq
on the set
\{S\subseteq[m]\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}|S|=2\}
and define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}=\mathcal{I}_{S}\setminus{\left(\bigcup_{S^{\prime}\subseteq[m]\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}|S^{\prime}|=2,S^{\prime}\preceq S}\mathcal{I}_{S^{\prime}}\right)}.

Observation 45
.

The collection of sets
\{\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}S\subseteq[m],|S|\leq 2\}
forms a partition of
B_{=1}
.

Lemma 46
.

Let
S\subseteq[m]
be such that
|S|\leq 2
. If
k\geq 2
and
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
is supported on
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}
then

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}\leq{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k}{2^{m/2-2}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle.

Proof.

We bound
{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,m-1,k}f\right\rangle\right|}\geq{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}
. This inequality is true because
R_{m,m,k}
and
R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k}
are PSD by. Suppose first that
|S|=1
so that
S=\{a\}
for some
a\in[m]
. Let
X\in\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}
. Then for every
W\subseteq[m]
with
|W|=m-1
and
a\in W
we have

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,W,k}}\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}\right]}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[k]}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,W,k}}{\left[\Delta{\left(\mathbf{Y}^{i},\mathbf{Y}^{j}\right)}=\{a\}\right]}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}}.

This is because
a\in W
, and so it must be the case that for all
i\neq j\in[k]
we have
X^{i}_{[m]\setminus\{b\}}\neq X^{j}_{[m]\setminus\{b\}}
. Otherwise we would have
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\{a,b\}}
, contradicting that
X\in\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\{a\}}
. Using this bound, we have that

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{W\subseteq[m],|W|=m-1}{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,W,k}f\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,m,k}f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m]\setminus\{a\},k}f\right\rangle\right|}+\frac{1}{m}\sum_{W\subseteq[m],|W|=m-1,a\in W}{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,W,k}f\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,m,k}f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\langle f,f\rangle+\frac{k}{2^{m/2-1}}\langle f,f\rangle+\frac{k}{2^{m/2}}\langle f,f\rangle.

The last inequality follows from the
Lemma

23
and our previous calculation, while noticing that any
R_{m,W,k}
has the uniform distribution over
\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
as a stationary distribution. A similar calculation shows that
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m,k}}\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}\right]}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m}}
, which is used to bound the third term
{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,m,k}f\right\rangle\right|}
by
Lemma

23
. This completes the proof for the case
S=\{a\}
.

Now assume that
|S|=2
so that
S=\{a,b\}
and
X\in\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}
. Fix
W\subseteq[m]
with
|W|=m-1
. Assume that
a\not\in W
. Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,W,k}}\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}\right]}\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[k]}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}^{m,W,k}_{X}}{\left[\Delta{\left(\mathbf{Y}^{i},\mathbf{Y}^{j}\right)}=b\right]}\leq\sum_{i,j\in[k]}\frac{1}{2^{m-1}}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}}.

This is because if
\Delta(X^{i},X^{j})=\{a\}
then
\Delta(\mathbf{Y}^{i},\mathbf{Y}^{j})=\{a\}\neq\{b\}
and otherwise
X^{i}_{[m]\setminus\{a\}}=X^{i}_{W}\neq X^{j}_{W}=X^{j}_{[m]\setminus\{a\}}
. Using this we find that

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m-1,k}}\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}\right]}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{W\subseteq[m],|S|=m-1}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,W,k}}\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}\right]}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}}.

If
b\in W
or
a,b\not\in W
a similar proof shows the same bound. Using the bound
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m,k}}\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{T}\right]}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}}
completes the proof:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}\leq{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,m-1,k}f\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,m,k}f\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k}{2^{m/2-1}}\langle f,f\rangle+\frac{k}{2^{m/2-1}}\langle f,f\rangle.

The second inequality is an application of
Lemma

23
.
∎

Lemma 47
.

Let
S\neq T\subseteq[m]
be such that
|S|,|T|\leq 2
. If
k\geq 2
and
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
is supported on
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}
and
g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
is supported on
\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{T}
then

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})g\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k}{2^{m/2-2}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

Proof.

Let
X\in\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{S}
. Let
a\in T\setminus S
be such that there does not exist
i,j\in[k]
such that
X\in\mathcal{I}_{\{a\}}
. Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m-1,k}}\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{T}\right]}\leq\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m-1,k}}\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}_{\{a\}}\right]}\leq\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m-1,k}}{\mathcal{I}}_{\{a\}}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j\in[k]}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,m-1,k}}{\left[\Delta{\left(\mathbf{Y}^{i},\mathbf{Y}^{j}\right)}=\{a\}\right]}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}}.

(
X\not\in\mathcal{I}_{\{a\}}
)

Then applying
Lemma

23
gives

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,m-1,k}g\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k}{2^{m/2-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

A similar calculation shows that
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m,k}}\widetilde{\mathcal{I}}\right]}\leq\frac{mk^{2}}{2^{m-1}}
. Then
Lemma

23
gives

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,m,k}g\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k}{2^{m/2-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

Applying the triangle inequality completes the proof.
∎

Corollary 48
.

Assume
k\geq 2
. Let
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
be supported on
B_{=1}
. Then

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}\leq{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{m^{5}k}{2^{m/2-2}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle.

Proof.

Write
f=\sum_{S\subseteq[m]\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}|S|\leq 2}f_{S}
where each
f_{S}
is supported on
\mathcal{I}_{S}
. Then

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,m-1,k}f\right\rangle\right|}

(
R_{m,m,k}
and
R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k}
both PSD)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{S,T\subseteq[m]\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}|S|,|T|\leq 2}{\left|\left\langle f_{S},R_{m,m-1,k}f_{T}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{S\subseteq[m]\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}|S|\leq 2}{\left|\left\langle f_{S},R_{m,m-1,k}f_{S}\right\rangle\right|}+\sum_{S\neq T\subseteq[m]\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}|S|,|T|\leq 2}{\left|\left\langle f_{S},R_{m,m-1,k}f_{T}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k}{2^{m/2-2}}\right)}\sum_{S\subseteq[m]\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}|S|\leq 2}\left\langle f_{S},f_{S}\right\rangle+\frac{k}{2^{m/2-2}}\sum_{S\neq T\subseteq[m]\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}|S|,|T|\leq 2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{S}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{T}\right\rVert}_{2}

(
Lemma

46
,
Lemma

47
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k}{2^{m/2-2}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle+\frac{k}{2^{m/2-2}}\sum_{S\neq T\subseteq[m]\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}|S|,|T|\leq 2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{S}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{T}\right\rVert}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k}{2^{m/2-2}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle+\frac{k}{2^{m/2-2}}\sum_{S\neq T\subseteq[m]\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}|S|,|T|\leq 2}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k}{2^{m/2-2}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle+\frac{m^{5}k^{2}}{2^{m/2-2}}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{m^{5}k}{2^{m/2-2}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle.

Note that we can apply
Lemma

46
and
Lemma

47
because
k\geq 2
.
∎

5.3
Cross Terms

We can use the same idea to bound the contributions from the cross terms.

Lemma 49
.

Let
f_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
be supported on
B_{=1}
and let
f_{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
be supported on
B_{\geq 2}
. Then

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f_{1},(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f_{2}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{\sqrt{m}k}{2^{m/2-2}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{1}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert}_{2}.

Proof.

For
X\in B_{\geq 2}
we have
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m-1,k}}B_{=1}\right]}\leq\frac{k^{2}m}{2^{m-1}}
. Apply
Lemma

23
to find that

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f_{1},R_{m,m-1,k}f_{2}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{\sqrt{m}k}{2^{m/2-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{1}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert}_{2}.

For
X\in B_{\geq 2}
we have
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m,k}}B_{=1}\right]}\leq\frac{k^{2}m}{2^{m-1}}
. Apply
Lemma

23
to find that

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f_{1},R_{m,m,k}f_{2}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{\sqrt{m}k}{2^{m/2-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{1}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert}_{2}.

Applying the triangle inequality completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 50
.

Let
f_{0}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
be supported on
B_{=0}
and let
f_{1}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
be supported on
B_{=1}\cup B_{\geq 2}
. Then

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f_{0},(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f_{1}\right\rangle\right|}=0.

Proof.

For
X\in B_{\geq 2}\cup B_{=1}
we have
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m-1,k}}B_{=0}\right]}=\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m,k}}B_{=0}\right]}=0
. Apply
Lemma

23
to bound

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f_{0},(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f_{1}\right\rangle\right|}\leq{\left|\left\langle f_{0},R_{m,m-1,k}f_{1}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f_{0},R_{m,m,k}f_{1}\right\rangle\right|}\leq 0.\qed

5.4
A Hybrid Argument for
f
Supported on
B_{\geq 2}

In this section we bound the square terms
\left\langle f_{2},(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f_{2}\right\rangle
for
f_{2}
supported on
B_{\geq 2}
. As mentioned in
Section

2
, our key idea is that when
k
is small compared to
m
, the fraction of
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
with two identical columns is so small that applying the noise by randomly permuting the rows is almost the same as randomly replacing the rows with completely random rows. That is, sampling without replacement resembles sampling with replacement closely.

This observation allows us to pass from the random walk described by
R_{m,m-1,k}
to a different random walk described by a nicer noise model described by operators we will call
Q_{m,m-1,k}
. The key tool we use is the bound given by
Lemma

21
for relating total-variation distances between Markov chain transition probabilities to a more linear-algebraic notion of closeness, stated in terms of their transition matrices. Fourier-analytic techniques will then be useful to bound the spectral norm of these
Q
-operators.

Definition 51
.

We define four random walk operators
7
7

7

R_{m,m-1,k}
and
R_{m,m,k}
have already been defined, but we define them again here for ease of comparison.

R_{m,m-1,k}
,
Q_{m,m-1,k}
,
R_{m,m,k}
, and
Q_{m,m,k}
on
\mathbb{R}^{\{\pm 1\}^{mk}}
.

•

Define

\displaystyle(R_{m,m-1,k}f)(X)

\displaystyle=\underset{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}}{\operatorname{{\bf E}}}{\left[f(\mathbf{Y})\right]}.

•

To define
Q_{m,m-1,k}
, for any
X\in{\left\{\pm 1\right\}}^{mk}
we define the distribution
\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}
as follows. To sample
\mathbf{Y}
from
\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}
, we sample
\mathbf{a}\in[m]
uniformly randomly and set
\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{\mathbf{a}}=X^{i}_{\mathbf{a}}
for all
i\in[k]
. Then set
\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{a}
uniformly randomly for
a\neq\mathbf{a}
. Then

\displaystyle(Q_{m,m-1,k}f)(X)

\displaystyle=\underset{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}}{\operatorname{{\bf E}}}{\left[f(\mathbf{Y})\right]}.

•

Define

\displaystyle(R_{m,m,k}f)(X)

\displaystyle=\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}^{m,m,k}_{X}}{\left[f(\mathbf{Y})\right]}.

•

The operator
Q_{m,m,k}
is defined by setting for each
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\left\{\pm 1\right\}}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}

\displaystyle(Q_{m,m,k}f)(X)

\displaystyle=\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathrm{Unif}{\left({\left\{\pm 1\right\}}^{mk}\right)}}{\left[f(\mathbf{Y})\right]}.

Fact 52
.

The matrices
Q_{m,m-1,k}
and
Q_{m,m,k}
are self-adjoint and PSD for any
m,k
.

We intend to show that
{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}
is small for
f
supported on
B_{\geq 2}
. We do so by a hybrid argument. In the following inequality, the first (and last) term on the RHS will be bounded by a simple bound on the total variation distance between the distribution
\mathcal{C}_{X}^{m,m-1,k}
and
\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,m-1,k}
(
\mathcal{C}_{X}^{m,m,k}
and
\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,m,k}
). As mentioned before, the second term on the RHS will be bounded using Fourier analysis.

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,m-1,k}-Q_{m,m-1,k}\right)}f\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(Q_{m,m,k}-Q_{m,m-1,k}\right)}f\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,m,k}-Q_{m,m,k}\right)}f\right\rangle\right|}.

5.4.1
The First Hybrid:
R_{m,m-1,k}
to
Q_{m,m-1,k}

Lemma 53
.

Assume that
k\leq 2^{m/3}
. For any
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
supported on
B_{\geq 2}
and we have that

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-Q_{m,m-1,k})f\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle.

Proof.

We directly compute (using the appropriate definitions of
p_{0}
and
p_{1}
given in
Lemma

21
):

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-Q_{m,m-1,k})f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{X\in B_{\geq 2}}f(X)^{2}\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m-1,k}}Y\right]}-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{Q_{m,m-1,k}}Y\right]}\right|}

(
Lemma

21
+ self-adjointness (
Fact

16
,
Fact

52
))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}}\sum_{X\in B_{\geq 2}}f(X)^{2}

(
Equation

3
below)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle.

It suffices to establish
Equation

3
. Assume that
X\in B_{\geq 2}
. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m-1,k}}Y\right]}-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{Q_{m,m-1,k}}Y\right]}\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a\in[m]}\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}}{\left[\mathbf{Y}=Y|a\text{ fixed}\right]}-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}}{\left[\mathbf{Y}=Y|a\text{ fixed}\right]}\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a\in[m]}\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2},Y_{a}=X_{a}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}}{\left[\mathbf{Y}=Y|a\text{ fixed}\right]}-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}}{\left[\mathbf{Y}=Y|a\text{ fixed}\right]}\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a\in[m]}\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2},Y_{a}=X_{a}}{\left|\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{m-1}-i}-\frac{1}{2^{(m-1)k}}\right|}

(
k\leq 2^{m/3}\leq 2^{m}-2
and
X\in B_{\geq 2}
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a\in[m]}\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2},Y_{a}=X_{a}}{\left|\frac{1}{2^{(m-1)k}}{\left(\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\frac{2^{m-1}}{2^{m-1}-i}-1\right)}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a\in[m]}\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2},Y_{a}=X_{a}}{\left|\frac{1}{2^{(m-1)k}}{\left(1+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}}-1\right)}\right|}

(
k\leq 2^{m/3}
,
Fact

22
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a\in[m]}\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2},Y_{a}=X_{a}}\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}2^{(m-1)k}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2},Y_{1}=X_{1}}\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}2^{(m-1)k}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2^{mk}}{2^{k}}\cdot\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}2^{(m-1)k}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}}.

(3)

Note that our computations for
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}}{\left[\mathbf{Y}=Y|a\text{ fixed}\right]}
and
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}}{\left[\mathbf{Y}=Y|a\text{ fixed}\right]}
relied on the fact that
Y\in B_{\geq 2}
.
∎

5.4.2
The Second Hybrid:
Q_{m,m-1,k}
to
Q_{m,m,k}

We use Fourier analysis to analyze the spectrum of the operator
Q_{m,m-1,k}-Q_{m,m,k}
, which will prove that
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert Q_{m,m-1,k}-Q_{m,m,k}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}
is small. We use the Fourier characters as an eigenbasis for
Q_{m,m-1,k}-Q_{m,m,k}
.

Fact 54
.

Fix
S_{1},\dots,S_{k}\subseteq[n]
. Then

\displaystyle{\left((Q_{m,m-1,k}-Q_{m,m,k})\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}\right)}=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\frac{1}{m}\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}&\text{ if }S_{1}\cup\dots\cup S_{k}=\{a\}\text{ for some $a\in[m]$}.\\
0&\text{ otherwise.}\end{cases}

Proof.

If
{\left|\bigcup_{i}S_{i}\right|}=1
then
S_{1}=\dots=S_{k}=\emptyset
and it is clear that

\displaystyle Q_{m,m-1,k}\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}=1=Q_{m,m,k}\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}.

Now assume
{\left|\bigcup_{i}S_{i}\right|}\geq 2
. Then for any
X=(X^{1},\dots,X^{k})\in\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
,

\displaystyle Q_{m,m-1,k}\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}(X^{1},\dots,X^{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}}{\left[\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a^{\prime}\in[m]}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}\\
\mathbf{a}=a^{\prime}\end{subarray}}{\left[\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a^{\prime}\in[m]}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}\\
\mathbf{a}=a^{\prime}\end{subarray}}{\left[\prod_{i\in[k]}\prod_{a\in S_{i}}\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{a}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a^{\prime}\in[m]}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}\\
\mathbf{a}=a^{\prime}\end{subarray}}{\left[{\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[k]\\
a^{\prime}\in S_{i}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{a^{\prime}}\right)}{\left(\prod_{i\in[k]}\prod_{a\in S_{i}\setminus\{a^{\prime}\}}\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{a}\right)}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a^{\prime}\in[m]}{\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[k]\\
a^{\prime}\in S_{i}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{a^{\prime}}\right)}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}\\
\mathbf{a}=a^{\prime}\end{subarray}}{\left[\prod_{i\in[k]}\prod_{a\in S_{i}\setminus\{a^{\prime}\}}\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{a}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a^{\prime}\in[m]}{\left(\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[k]\\
a^{\prime}\in S_{i}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{a^{\prime}}\right)}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\in{\left(\{\pm 1\}^{n}\right)}^{[k]\setminus\{a^{\prime}\}}}{\left[\chi_{S_{1}\setminus\{a^{\prime}\},\dots,S_{k}\setminus\{a^{\prime}\}}(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

(since at least one of the
S_{i}\setminus\{a^{\prime}\}
is nonempty)

It is easy to see that
Q_{m,m,k}\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}=0
, since
S_{1}\cup\dots\cup S_{k}\neq\emptyset
.

Now assume that
\bigcup_{i}S_{i}=\{a^{\prime}\}
for some
a^{\prime}\in[m]
. Then it is clear that
Q_{m,m,k}\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}=0
, since
S_{1}\cup\dots\cup S_{k}\neq\emptyset
. We now compute

\displaystyle Q_{m,m-1,k}\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}(X^{1},\dots,X^{k})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}}{\left[\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{a\in[m]}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}\\
\mathbf{a}=a\end{subarray}}{\left[\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[k]\\
S_{i}=\{a^{\prime}\}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{Y}^{k}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}\\
\mathbf{a}=a^{\prime}\end{subarray}}{\left[\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[k]\\
S_{i}=\{a^{\prime}\}\end{subarray}}\mathbf{Y}^{k}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}

(If
\mathbf{a}\neq a^{\prime}
then the
\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{a^{\prime}}
are uniformly random.)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\begin{subarray}{c}(\mathbf{Y}^{1},\dots,\mathbf{Y}^{k})\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m-1,k}_{X}\\
\mathbf{a}=a^{\prime}\end{subarray}}{\left[\prod_{\begin{subarray}{c}i\in[k]\\
S_{i}=\{a^{\prime}\}\end{subarray}}X^{k}_{a^{\prime}}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}(X^{1},\dots,X^{k}).

Therefore
{\left(Q_{m,m-1,k}-Q_{m,m,k}\right)}\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}=\frac{1}{m}\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}(X^{1},\dots,X^{k})-0=\frac{1}{m}\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}(X^{1},\dots,X^{k})
.
∎

Corollary 55
.

Let
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
. Then

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(Q_{m,m-1,k}-Q_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{1}{m}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle.

Proof.

The
\chi_{S_{1},\dots,S_{k}}
form an orthonormal eigenbasis (
Fact

20
) for
Q_{m,m-1,k}-Q_{m,m,k}
, and by
Fact

54
each basis element has eigenvalue with absolute value at most
\frac{1}{m}
.
∎

5.4.3
The Third Hybrid:
Q_{m,m,k}
to
R_{m,m,k}

Lemma 56
.

Assume that
k\leq 2^{m/3}
. For any
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
supported on
B_{\geq 2}
we have

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m,k}-Q_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}^{2}.

Proof.

As in the proof of
Lemma

53
we find that (with the appropriate definitions of
p_{0}
and
p_{1}
for use of
Lemma

21
,

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m,k}-Q_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{X\in B_{\geq 2}}f(X)^{2}\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m,k}}Y\right]}-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{Q_{m,m,k}}Y\right]}\right|}

(
Lemma

21
+ self-adjointness (
Fact

16
,
Fact

52
))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m}}\sum_{X\in B_{\geq 2}}f(X)^{2}

(
Equation

4
below)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m}}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle.

It remains to prove
Equation

4
:

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,m,k}}Y\right]}-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{Q_{m,m,k}}Y\right]}\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}^{m,m,k}_{X}}{\left[\mathbf{Y}=Y\right]}-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,m,k}_{X}}{\left[\mathbf{Y}=Y\right]}\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2}}{\left|\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{m}-i}-\frac{1}{2^{mk}}\right|}

(
k\leq 2^{m/3}\leq 2^{m}-2
and
X\in B_{\geq 2}
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2}}{\left|\frac{1}{2^{mk}}{\left(\prod_{i=0}^{k-1}\frac{2^{m}}{2^{m}-i}-1\right)}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2}}{\left|\frac{1}{2^{mk}}{\left(1+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m}}-1\right)}\right|}

(
k\leq 2^{m/3}
,
Fact

22
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 2}}\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m}2^{mk}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{2^{mk}}\cdot\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m}2^{mk}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m}}.

(4)

Note that our computations relied on the fact that
Y\in B_{\geq 2}
.
∎

5.4.4
Putting Hybrids Together

Proposition 57
.

Assume that
k\leq 2^{m/3}
. For any
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
supported on
B_{\geq 2}
, we have

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}\leq{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m/2}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle

Proof.

By the triangle inequality,

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,m-1,k}-R_{m,m,k})f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,m-1,k}-Q_{m,m-1,k}\right)}f\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(Q_{m,m,k}-Q_{m,m-1,k}\right)}f\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,m,k}-Q_{m,m,k}\right)}f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-1}}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle+\frac{1}{m}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m}}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle

(
Lemma

53
,
Corollary

55
,
Lemma

56
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{1}{m}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{m-2}}\right)}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle.

Note we can apply
Lemma

53
and
Lemma

56
because
f
is supported on
B_{\geq 2}
.
∎

6
Proof of
Theorem

29

In this section let
m
and
k\leq 2^{m}-2
be fixed positive integers. As in the proof of
Theorem

25
(via its restatement
Theorem

42
) we will break
\mathbb{R}^{\{\pm 1\}^{mk}}
into orthogonal components and bound the contributions of each of these cross terms to evaluation on the quadratic form given by
R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}
.

Define the following subsets of
\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
:

•

B_{=0}={\left\{X\in\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\exists i\neq j\in[k],X^{i}=X^{j}\right\}}
.
8
8

8

B_{=0}
was already defined in
Section

5
but we define it again here for convenience.

•

B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}={\left\{X\in\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\setminus B_{=0}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\exists i\neq j\in[k],X^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}=X^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}\right\}}
.

•

B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}={\left\{X\in\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\forall i\neq j\in[k],X^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}\neq X^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}\right\}}
.

Note that these sets form a partition of
\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
.

As in the proof of
Theorem

25
, the contribution from the parts of functions supported on
B_{=0}
will be bounded by induction. The component
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
will play the role that
B_{\geq 2}
did: the part of the domain on which the noise model induced by random permutations (sampling without replacement) to the bits in
[m-\ell-1,m-1]
is close to the noise model induced by completely random replacement of bits (sampling with replacement).
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}
is the component on which these two noise models are not similar, but as in the case of
B_{=1}
, this set will already show good expansion.

The following is equivalent to
Theorem

29
because for any
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
we have that

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}=

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert{\left(R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}\right)}^{*}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}.

To bound this quantity, we can use that

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f\right\rVert}^{2}_{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f,{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}{\left(R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}\right)}^{*}f,f\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}^{2}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f,f\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}-R_{m,[m],k}+R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f,f\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f\right\rangle.

Theorem 58

(
Theorem

29
restated)
.

Fix any
m\geq 100
and set
100\leq\ell\leq m
. Suppose
k\leq 2^{\ell/10}
. Then we have for any
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
that

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k^{3}}{2^{\ell/4-60}}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle\leq\frac{1}{\ell^{2}}\left\langle f,f\right\rangle.

Proof.

We prove by induction on
k
. In the base case
k=1
and the result holds by the following argument when we write
f=f_{2}
. Now assume that the result holds for real functions on
\{\pm 1\}^{m(k-1)}
.

Let
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
. Write
f=f_{0}+f_{1}+f_{2}
where
f_{0}
is supported on
B_{=0}
,
f_{1}
is supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}
, and
f_{2}
is supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
. By
Lemma

50
applied with
R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}
,
R_{m,[m-1],k}
and
R_{m,[m],k}
and
B_{=0}
and
\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\setminus B_{=0}=B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}\cup B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
, the other cross terms vanish, and we have

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,Af\right\rangle\right|}

(Defining
A
for convenience of notation)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f_{0},Af_{0}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f_{2},Af_{2}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f_{1},A(f_{1}+f_{2})\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f_{2},Af_{1}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f_{0},Af_{0}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f_{2},Af_{2}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f_{1},A(f_{1}+f_{2})\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f_{1},A^{*}f_{2}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{(k-1)^{3}}{2^{\ell/4-60}}\left\langle f_{0},f_{0}\right\rangle+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/4-20}}\langle f_{2},f_{2}\rangle+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/2-4}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{1}+f_{2}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert}_{2}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/2-4}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{1}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{2}\right\rVert}_{2}

(
Lemma

40
+ Induction,
Corollary

64
,
Corollary

61
,
Corollary

61
, in that order)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{3}}{2^{\ell/4-60}}\langle f,f\rangle.

In the last step we used that
k\leq 2^{\ell/10}
.
∎

Similar to
Section

5.4
we will again argue that because
\ell
is large, applying a random permutation to the same indices of all elements of a tuple of binary strings is similar to replacing those indices with random binary strings in all elements of a tuple. To model this situation, we similarly use the matrix
Q_{m,S,k}
to denote the random walk matrix induced by the following distribution
\mathcal{C}_{X}^{m,S,k}
for each
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
. To draw
\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{C}_{X}^{m,S,k}
, set
\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{[m]\setminus S}=X^{i}_{[m]\setminus S}
and set
\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{S}
uniformly at random for all
i\in[k]
.

6.1
f
Supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}
and Cross Terms

The first step in comparing the noise model generated by the application of a random permutation to a substring of each string in a
k
-tuple of
n
-bit strings is showing that the set of strings to which this comparison fails already exhibits good expansion.

In this section we leverage that
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}
is a set in
\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
with very good expansion. For intuition,
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}
plays the role that
B_{=1}
played in
Section

5
. The following lemma formalizes the expansion property that we need.

Lemma 59
.

For any
X\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}\cup B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
we have that for any
I,J\subseteq[m]
such that
I\cup J=[m]
and
I,J\supseteq[m-\ell-1,m-1]
,

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,I,k}R_{m,J,k}}B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}\right]}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-3}}.

Moreover, we also have

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,I,k}R_{m,J,k}R_{m,I,k}}B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}\right]}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-3}}.

In fact, this holds with
Q
replacing
R
anywhere in this inequality.

Proof.

We first prove the first statement (with two steps) for the case of drawing from the distribution
\mathcal{D}
(corresponding to the random walk operator
R
), but the proof translates easily to the case for
\mathcal{C}
(corresponding to the random wak operator
Q
). Model the process as first drawing
\bm{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}^{m,I,k}_{X}
and then
\bm{Z}\sim\mathcal{D}^{m,J,k}_{\bm{Y}}
. Fix any
X\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}\cup B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
. Define the following subset of pairs of indices in
\binom{[k]}{2}
:

\displaystyle P_{1}=

\displaystyle{\left\{\{i,j\}\in\binom{[k]}{2}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}X^{i}_{I}=X^{j}_{I}\right\}}.

Assume that
\{i,j\}\in P_{1}
. Then
\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{I}=\mathbf{Y}^{j}_{I}
with probability 1. But now note that
X^{i}_{J}\neq X^{j}_{J}
since otherwise
X^{i}=X^{j}
, which contradicts that
X\not\in B_{=0}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,I,k}\\
\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{m,J,k}\end{subarray}}{\left[\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}=\mathbf{Z}^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,J,k}\end{subarray}}{\left[\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}=\mathbf{Z}^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{\ell}-1}.

(5)

Now assume that
\{i,j\}\not\in P_{1}
. Let
E
be the event that
\mathbf{Y}^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}=\mathbf{Y}^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}
. Then

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,I,k}\\
\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{m,J,k}\end{subarray}}{\left[\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}=\mathbf{Z}^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,I,k}}{\left[E\right]}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,I,k}\\
\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{m,J,k}\end{subarray}}{\left[\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}=\mathbf{Z}^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}|E\right]}

\displaystyle+\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,I,k}}{\left[\overline{E}\right]}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,I,k}\\
\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{m,J,k}\end{subarray}}{\left[\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}=\mathbf{Z}^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}|\overline{E}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{\ell}}+\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,I,k}\\
\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{m,J,k}\end{subarray}}{\left[\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}=\mathbf{Z}^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}|\overline{E}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{\ell}}+\frac{1}{2^{\ell}-1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{\ell-2}}.

(6)

To complete the proof, we use a union bound and find

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,I,k}\\
\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{m,J,k}\end{subarray}}{\left[\mathbf{Z}\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\{i,j\}\in\binom{[k]}{2}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,I,k}\\
\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{m,J,k}\end{subarray}}{\left[\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}=\mathbf{Z}^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\{i,j\}\in P_{1}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,I,k}\\
\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{m,J,k}\end{subarray}}{\left[\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}=\mathbf{Z}^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}\right]}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\{i,j\}\not\in P_{1}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{m,I,k}\\
\mathbf{Z}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathbf{Y}}^{m,J,k}\end{subarray}}{\left[\mathbf{Z}^{i}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}=\mathbf{Z}^{j}_{[m-\ell-1,m-1]}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\{i,j\}\in P_{1}}\frac{1}{2^{\ell}}+\sum_{\{i,j\}\not\in P_{1}}\frac{1}{2^{\ell-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\{i,j\}\in\binom{[k]}{2}}\frac{1}{2^{\ell-2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-2}}.

(7)

To prove the second statement (with three steps taken), note that the probability that a random permutation applied to the bits in
I
sends an element of
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
to an element of
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}
is at most
\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell}-1}
. Applying a union bound and the bound
Equation

7
completes the proof.
∎

The expansion property just proved results in the following linear-algebraic statements, which show that the contributions from
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}
to our operator norm bound is extremely small.

Lemma 60
.

Let
k\geq 2
and
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
be supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}
and
g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
be supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}\cup B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}
. Let
I
and
J
be such that
I\cup J=[m]
. Then

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,J,k}R_{m,I,k}R_{m,J,k}g\right\rangle\right|}\leq\sqrt{\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-3}}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2},

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,J,k}R_{m,I,k}g\right\rangle\right|}\leq\sqrt{\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-3}}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

Moreover, this holds with
Q
in place of
R
anywhere.

Proof.

The inequality directly follows from
Lemma

23
and
Lemma

59
. We can apply
Lemma

23
because the uniform distribution on
\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
is indeed a stationary distribution under
R_{m,S,k}
for any
S
(
Fact

17
). The
Q
case follows from the fact that
Lemma

59
applies for the distributions
\mathcal{C}
too.
∎

Corollary 61
.

Let
k\geq 2
and
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
be supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}
and
g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
be supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}\cup B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}
. Then

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k}{2^{\ell/2-4}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2},

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}g\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k}{2^{\ell/2-4}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

Proof.

We compute

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}R_{m,[m-1],k}}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,{R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}R_{m,[m],k}}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}R_{m,[m-1],k}}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,{R_{m,[m],k}}g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k}{2^{\ell/2-3}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}+\frac{k}{2^{\ell/2}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}

(
Lemma

60
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k}{2^{\ell/2-4}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

The proof of the second statement is similar.
∎

6.2
A Hybrid Argument for
f
Supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}

The role that
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
plays in this section is similar to the role played by
B_{\geq 2}
in
Section

5
. It is the region of
\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
that is “well-behaved” in the sense that the nicer noise model given by the
Q
operators is similar to the noise model given by the
R
operators on this region of
\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
.

In particular, because these noise models behave similarly when restricted to these
k
-tuples, we can bound the difference between the corresponding transition matrices as follows:

Lemma 62
.

Assume that
k\leq 2^{\ell/10}
and
f,g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}
be supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
. Then for any
S\supseteq[m-\ell-1,m-1]
, we have

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(Q_{m,S,k}-R_{m,S,k}\right)}g\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

Proof.

By assumption
f
and
g
are supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathsf{safe}}
. Therefore, by
Lemma

21
and self-adjointness of
Q_{m,S,k}
and
R_{m,S,k}
(
Fact

16
,
Fact

52
) we have

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,(R_{m,S,k}-Q_{m,S,k})g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{X\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}}f(X)^{2}\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,S,k}}Y\right]}-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{Q_{m,S,k}}Y\right]}\right|}}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;\cdot\sqrt{\sum_{X\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}}g(X)^{2}\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,S,k}}Y\right]}-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{Q_{m,S,k}}Y\right]}\right|}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\sqrt{\sum_{X\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}}f(X)^{2}}\sqrt{\sum_{X\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}}g(X)^{2}}

(
Equation

8
below)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

Here
p_{0}
and
p_{1}
are as used below. Now it suffices to establish
Equation

8
. Assume
X\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
. Then because
S\supseteq[m-\ell-1,m-1]
, we know that for all
i\neq j
we have
X^{i}_{S}\neq X^{j}_{S}
.

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,S,k}}Y\right]}-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{Q_{m,S,k}}Y\right]}\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}\\
\forall i\in[k],a\in[m]\setminus S,Y^{i}_{a}=X^{i}_{a}\end{subarray}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}^{m,S,k}_{X}}{\left[\mathbf{Y}=Y\right]}-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{C}^{m,S,k}_{X}}{\left[\mathbf{Y}=Y\right]}\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}\\
\forall i\in[k],a\in[m]\setminus S,Y^{i}_{a}=X^{i}_{a}\end{subarray}}{\left|\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{1}{2^{|S|}-j}-\frac{1}{2^{|S|k}}\right|}

(
k\leq 2^{m/3}\leq 2^{m}-2
and
X,Y\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}\\
\forall i\in[k],a\in[m]\setminus S,Y^{i}_{a}=X^{i}_{a}\end{subarray}}{\left|\frac{1}{2^{|S|k}}{\left(\prod_{j=0}^{k-1}\frac{2^{|S|}}{2^{|S|}-j}-1\right)}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}\\
\forall i\in[k],a\in[m]\setminus S,Y^{i}_{a}=X^{i}_{a}\end{subarray}}{\left|\frac{1}{2^{|S|k}}{\left(1+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{|S|}}-1\right)}\right|}

(
k^{2}\leq 2^{\ell}\leq 2^{|S|}
,
Fact

22
)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}\\
\forall i\in[k],a\in[m]\setminus S,Y^{i}_{a}=X^{i}_{a}\end{subarray}}\frac{k^{2}}{2^{|S|}2^{|S|k}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\cdot 2^{|S|k}\cdot\frac{k^{2}}{2^{|S|}2^{|S|k}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{|S|}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}.

(8)

The last inequality follows because
|S|\geq{\left|[m-\ell-1,m-1]\right|}=\ell-1
. Having established
Equation

8
, we have completed the proof.
∎

At this point it may seem like we are essentially finished with the proof, since we should just replace the
R
operators in the expression
\left\langle f,{R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f\right\rangle
with the corresponding
Q
operators and finish the proof. However, we don’t quite show an upper bound on the
operator norm
of
R-Q
. Rather, we simply show that they are close on the well-behaved region. A priori, this gives us no information about how products of these operators may behave, since the first term in the product may “rotate” vectors into the badly-behaved region. So a straightforward application of the triangle inequality fails.

However, we observe that we have already shown in
Section

6.1
that random walks starting outside the badly-behaved region rarely transition into it, so that we almost can pretend as if all of these operators are operators on
\mathbb{R}^{B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}}
. We formalize this by inserting projections to the space of functions supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
, and showing that this move does very little quantitatively.

Lemma 63
.

We have for any
f,g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
that

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}-Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}\right)}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}g\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/4-20}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

Moreover, we have for such
f
and
g
that

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}+Q_{m,[m],k}\right)}g\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/4-20}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

Proof.

Let
\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}
be the projection to
\{h\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}h\text{ supported on }B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}\}
. We directly compute

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}-Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}-Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}\right)}{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\;\;+{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}-Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}\right)}\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}-Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}\right)}{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rangle\right|}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rVert}_{2}

(
Lemma

62
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rangle\right|}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rVert}_{2}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/4-10}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

(
Equation

9
below)

Here our application of the
Lemma

62
depended on the fact that
\mathrm{Supp}(\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}R_{m,[m-1],k}f)\subseteq B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
and
\mathrm{Supp}((\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}})R_{m,[m-1],k}f)\subseteq\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\setminus B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
.

To establish
Equation

9
we compute

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rVert}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}g,{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle R_{m,[m-1],k}g,{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}^{2}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-1],k}{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rangle

(self-adjointness (
Fact

16
))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k}{2^{\ell/2-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}g\right\rVert}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k}{2^{\ell/2-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}^{2}.

(9)

The first inequality follows from
Lemma

23
the fact that
\mathrm{Supp}(f)\subseteq B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
and for any
X\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
, we have
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{R_{m,[m-1],k}}B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}\right]}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}
(using the same proof as
Lemma

59
), and that
\mathrm{Supp}{\left({\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}R_{m,[m-1],k}f\right)}\subseteq\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\setminus B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
. Bounding the similar quantity but with
R_{m,[m],k}
instead of
R_{m,[m-1],k}
is the same and the first part of the lemma statement follows from the triangle inequality.

The second part of the lemma statement follows from the same argument and an application of the triangle inequality:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}+Q_{m,[m],k}\right)}g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}+Q_{m,[m],k}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}

(
Fact

16
,
Fact

52
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m-1],k}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m],k}-Q_{m,[m],k}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}.

(10)

We show how to bound the first term in this sum:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m-1],k}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m-1],k}\right)}{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\;\;+{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m-1],k}\right)}\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m-1],k}\right)}{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rVert}_{2}

(
Lemma

62
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-1],k}{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle+{\left|\left\langle f,Q_{m,[m-1],k}{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle\right|}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rVert}_{2}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k}{2^{\ell/4-10}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}.

(
Equation

11
below)

To establish
Equation

11
we compute

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rVert}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left\langle g,Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rangle

(self-adjointness (
Fact

52
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k}{2^{\ell/2-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert{\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}g\right\rVert}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k}{2^{\ell/2-1}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g\right\rVert}_{2}^{2}.

(11)

The first inequality follows from
Lemma

23
the fact that
\mathrm{Supp}(f)\subseteq B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
and for any
X\in B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
, we have
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}{\left[X\to_{Q_{m,[m-1],k}}B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{coll}}\right]}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell-1}}
(using the same proof as
Lemma

59
). The bound on the second term in the sum
Equation

10
follows from the same argument.
∎

Corollary 64
.

Let
k\geq 2
and
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{mk}\to\mathbb{R}
be supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
. Then

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/4-50}}\langle f,f\rangle.

Proof.

Let
g=R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f
and we can write
g=g_{1}+g_{2}
where
g_{1}=\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}g
and
g_{2}={\left(\mathrm{Id}-\Pi_{\mathrm{safe}}\right)}g
. Then

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}g_{1}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}g_{2}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}g_{1}\right\rangle\right|}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/2-3}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g_{2}\right\rVert}_{2}.

(
Corollary

61
)

To bound the first term, we note that
g_{1}
is supported on
B_{\geq 1}^{\mathrm{safe}}
and directly compute

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}g_{1}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}g_{1}\right\rangle\right|}+

\displaystyle\;\;{\left|\left\langle f,{\left(R_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}-Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}\right)}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}g_{1}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle f,Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}\right)}g_{1}\right\rangle\right|}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/4-20}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g_{1}\right\rVert}_{2}

(
Lemma

63
, first part)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/2-20}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g_{1}\right\rVert}_{2}+{\left|\left\langle f,Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(Q_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m],k}\right)}f\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\;\;\;+{\left|\left\langle f,Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}+Q_{m,[m],k}\right)}g_{1}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/4-20}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g_{1}\right\rVert}_{2}+{\left|\left\langle f,Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(R_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m-1],k}-R_{m,[m],k}+Q_{m,[m],k}\right)}g_{1}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/4-20}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g_{1}\right\rVert}_{2}+\frac{k^{2}}{2^{\ell/4-20}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g_{1}\right\rVert}_{2}.

(
Lemma

63
, second part)

For the second-to-last equality we used that
Q_{m,[m-\ell-1,m],k}{\left(Q_{m,[m-1],k}-Q_{m,[m],k}\right)}=0
because
Q_{m,S,k}Q_{m,T,k}=Q_{m,S\cup T,k}
for any
S,T\subseteq[m]
.

To complete the proof we use that
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g_{1}\right\rVert}_{2},\mathinner{\!\left\lVert g_{2}\right\rVert}_{2}\leq\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f\right\rVert}_{2}
.
∎

7
Reduction from Two-Dimensional to One-Dimensional Constructions: Proof of
Theorem

4

7.1
Definitions

To prove
Theorem

4
we introduce some new notation required for dealing with bit arrays on a higher-dimensional lattice architecture.

7.1.1
Bit Arrays

We regard an element
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
as a function
x\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}\times{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}\to\{\pm 1\}
.
Similarly, we regard an element
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
as a function
X\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}\times{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}\times{\left[k\right]}\to\{\pm 1\}
.
For
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
, and
i,j\in{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}
, and
\ell\in[k]
, we use the notation:

•

X^{\ell}_{i,j}=X(i,j,\ell)\in\{\pm 1\}

•

X^{\ell}=X\mid_{{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}\times{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}\times\{\ell\}}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}

•

X^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}=X\mid_{\{i\}\times{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}\times\{\ell\}}\in\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}

•

X^{\ell}_{\cdot,j}=X\mid_{{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}\times\{j\}\times\{\ell\}}\in\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}

•

X_{i,\cdot}=X\mid_{\{i\}\times{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}\times[k]}\in\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}

•

X_{\cdot,j}=X\mid_{{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}\times\{j\}\times[k]}\in\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}

We will use
{\mathscr{D}}_{n}^{(k)}
to denote the set of all
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
such that
X^{i}\neq X^{j}
when
i\neq j
. Unless otherwise specified,
{\mathscr{D}}
refers to
{\mathscr{D}}_{n}^{(k)}
.

7.1.2
Color Classes

We partition
\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
into “color classes” via the following relation. Let
R^{(\sqrt{n})}
be a tuple of
\sqrt{n}
equivalence relations on
[k]
. That is, there is one equivalence relation for each row in
[\sqrt{n}]
. Then we define:

B_{R^{(\sqrt{n})}}=\left\{X\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\forall i\in\sqrt{n},X_{i,\cdot}^{\ell}=X_{i,\cdot}^{m}\text{ if and only if }\ell\,R_{i}\,m\right\}.

Informally,
X^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}
and
X^{m}_{i,\cdot}
share a color if
X^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}=X^{m}_{i,\cdot}
. This relation induces a coloring on the rows of
X
. We then say that
X
and
Y
are colored the same if all of their rows are colored the same. Since
R^{(\sqrt{n})}
is an equivalence relation itself, the sets
\{B_{R^{(\sqrt{n})}}\}_{R^{(\sqrt{n})}\in{\mathscr{R}}^{\otimes\sqrt{n}}}
partition
\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
for
{\mathscr{R}}
the set of equivalence relations on
[k]
. Additionally, each
X
has a unique color class we will denote as
B(X)
. We will also use
{\mathscr{B}}
to denote the set of color classes. This partition is useful in part due to its size.

Fact 65
.

There are
\leq k^{k\sqrt{n}}
color classes, that is,
{\left|{\mathscr{B}}\right|}\leq k^{k\sqrt{n}}
.

Proof.

We can count each color class by identifying the partition of each row, of which there are
\sqrt{n}
. Each row consists of
k
elements, so we can overcount the number as putting the
k
elements into
k
partitions,
k^{k}
.
∎

We will define a simpler partition that will facilitate much of our analysis:

\displaystyle B_{\text{safe}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\left\{X\in{\mathscr{D}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\forall\ell\neq m\in[k],i\in[\sqrt{n}],X^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}\neq X^{m}_{i,\cdot}\right\}},

\displaystyle B_{\text{coll}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathscr{D}}\setminus B_{\text{safe}},

\displaystyle B_{=0}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{\pm 1\}^{nk}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}.

That is,
B_{\text{safe}}
is the color class determined by the
\sqrt{n}
-wise product of the identity relation.
B_{\text{coll}}
then consists of all other color classes within
{\mathscr{D}}
, whereas
B_{=0}
consists of all elements outside of
{\mathscr{D}}
. We will often use the following result on the size of
B_{\text{coll}}
:

Fact 66
.

\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}\leq\frac{2\sqrt{n}k^{2}}{2^{\sqrt{n}}}
.

Proof.

We may write:

\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}=\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|\{\pm 1\}^{nk}\right|}}\cdot\frac{{\left|\{\pm 1\}^{nk}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}.

The first can be viewed as the probability of sampling an element of
B_{\mathrm{coll}}
when sampling from
\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
. The process of sampling from
\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
can be seen as sampling
\sqrt{n}k
rows from
\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}
. Under this view, a simple union bound tells us that there are at most
\sqrt{n}k^{2}
possible “collisions” that would induce a non-distinct color class, allowing us to bound the probability by
\frac{\sqrt{n}k^{2}}{2^{\sqrt{n}}}
.

For the other term, we will prove simply that
\frac{{\left|B_{=0}\right|}}{{\left|\{\pm 1\}^{nk}\right|}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
. Note that our analysis above actually bounds the probability
X\sim\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
is not in
B_{\mathrm{safe}}
, which is more than sufficient for this. ∎

7.1.3
Distributions

Because in this section we are dealing with permutations of
n
-bit strings where the bits lie on a higher-dimensional lattice, it will be convenient to introduce completely new notation that will supplant the random walk operators
R_{n,S,k}
from before.

If
\pi\in\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
, then let
\pi^{\otimes k}\in\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}
be such that
\pi^{\otimes k}(X)^{\ell}=\pi(X^{\ell})
for all
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
and
\ell\in[k]
.

•

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{P}_{R}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
such that
\pi\sim\mathcal{P}_{R}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\sigma_{i}\sim\mathcal{B}
independently for each
i\in{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}
and define
\pi
such that
\pi(x)_{i,\cdot}=\sigma_{i}(x_{i,\cdot})
for all
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
and all
i\in{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{P}_{C}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
such that
\pi\sim\mathcal{P}_{C}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\sigma_{i}\sim\mathcal{B}
independently for each
i\in{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}
and define
\pi
such that
\pi(x)_{\cdot,i}=\sigma_{i}(x_{\cdot,i})
for all
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
and all
i\in{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{P}^{0}=\mathcal{P}_{R}
. For all
t\geq 1
, let
\mathcal{P}^{t}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
such that
\pi\sim\mathcal{P}^{t}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\sigma_{1}\sim\mathcal{P}^{t-1}
,
\sigma_{2}\sim\mathcal{P}_{C}
, and
\sigma_{3}\sim\mathcal{P}_{R}
and define
\pi
such that
\pi(x)=(\sigma_{3}\circ\sigma_{2}\circ\sigma_{1})(x)
for all
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
. It is worth noting that this construction is exactly that of our circuit model above.

•

Let
\mathcal{G}_{R}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
such that
\pi\sim\mathcal{G}_{R}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\sigma_{i}\sim\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}})
independently for each
i\in{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}
and define
\pi
such that
\pi(x)_{i,\cdot}=\sigma_{i}(x_{i,\cdot})
for all
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{G}_{C}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
such that
\pi\sim\mathcal{G}_{C}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\sigma_{i}\sim\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}})
independently for each
i\in{\left[\sqrt{n}\,\right]}
and define
\pi
such that
\pi(x)_{\cdot,i}=\sigma_{i}(x_{\cdot,i})
for all
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{G}^{0}=\mathcal{G}_{R}
. For all
t\geq 1
, let
\mathcal{G}^{t}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
such that
\pi\sim\mathcal{G}^{t}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\sigma_{1}\sim\mathcal{G}^{t-1}
,
\sigma_{2}\sim\mathcal{G}_{C}
, and
\sigma_{3}\sim\mathcal{G}_{R}
and define
\pi
such that
\pi(x)=(\sigma_{3}\circ\sigma_{2}\circ\sigma_{1})(x)
for all
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{G}
be
\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}})
, i.e. the uniform distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
.

•

If
\mathcal{D}
is a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
and
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
, let
\mathcal{D}^{(k)}_{X}
be a distribution on
\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
such that
Y\sim\mathcal{D}^{(k)}_{X}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\pi\sim\mathcal{D}
and define
Y
such that
Y=\pi^{\otimes k}(X)
.
Note that if
X\in{\mathscr{D}}
, then
\mathcal{G}^{(k)}_{X}
is
\mathcal{U}{\left({\mathscr{D}}\right)}
. If the superscript is understood from the context of
X
to be
k
, we will often drop it and just write
\mathcal{D}_{X}
.

It will be helpful to think of the distribution
\mathcal{D}
as defining a Markov chain on
\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
for any choice
k\geq 1
. More specifically,
\mathcal{D}^{(k)}_{X}
can be thought of as specifying the transition probabilities out of
X
in the corresponding Markov chain on
\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
. The transition operators corresponding to these Markov chains are given by:

\displaystyle(T_{\mathcal{D}}f)(X)=\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\mathbf{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{(k)}}{\left[f(\mathbf{Y})\right]}=\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\bm{Y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{X}^{(k)}}[\bm{Y}=Y]\cdot f(Y).

When the value of
k
is not clear from context, we will write
T_{\mathcal{D}^{(k)}}
for this operator.

7.2
Linear Algebra

Recall that
\mathcal{D}_{X}
can be thought of as the distribution after a 1-step random walk from
X
according to a permutation drawn from
\mathcal{D}
.

Fact 67
.

T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}
,
T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}
, and
T_{\mathcal{G}}
are self-adjoint w.r.t our inner product.

Proof.

We will state the proof for
T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}
, the reasoning for the others being symmetric. Let
f,g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{nk}\to\mathbb{R}
.

\displaystyle\langle f,T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}g\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{X\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}f(X)(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}g)(X)

\displaystyle=\sum_{X\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}f(X)\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Y]\cdot g(Y)

\displaystyle=\sum_{X\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}f(X)\cdot g(Y)\cdot\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Y].

Note the symmetry in above: we are done if we prove that
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Y]=\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[Y\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}X]
. This fact is quite observable from the definition of
\mathcal{G}_{R}
, since for any
\sigma\in\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
the probability it is drawn from
\mathcal{G}_{R}
is the same as the probability of drawing
\sigma^{-1}
.
∎

Fact 68
.

Let
\mathcal{D}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
. Then:

T_{\mathcal{D}}T_{\mathcal{G}}=T_{\mathcal{G}}=T_{\mathcal{G}}T_{\mathcal{D}}.

Fact 69
.

Let
U_{1},...,U_{s}
and
W_{1},...,W_{s}
be operators. Then we have:

\prod_{i=1}^{s}U_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{s}W_{i}=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}U_{j}\cdot{\left(U_{i}-W_{i}\right)}\cdot\prod_{j=i+1}^{s}W_{j}.

Proof.

We prove this by induction on
s
. The base case
s=1
is immediate. For the inductive hypothesis note that:

\displaystyle\prod_{i=1}^{s+1}U_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{s+1}W_{i}

\displaystyle={\left(\prod_{i=1}^{s}U_{i}-\prod_{i=1}^{s}W_{i}\right)}W_{s+1}+\prod_{i=1}^{s}U_{i}{\left(U_{s+1}-W_{s+1}\right)}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{s}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}U_{j}\cdot{\left(U_{i}-W_{i}\right)}\cdot\prod_{j=i+1}^{s+1}W_{j}+\prod_{i=1}^{s}U_{i}{\left(U_{s+1}-W_{s+1}\right)}

(Induction)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{s+1}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}U_{j}\cdot{\left(U_{i}-W_{i}\right)}\cdot\prod_{j=i+1}^{s+1}W_{j}.

This completes the induction and the proof.
∎

7.3
Proof of
Theorem

4

In this section we will prove
Theorem

4
, reducing the result to a spectral norm bound to be proved in
Section

8
. Our key insight will be that given the distribution and operator framework outlined in the previous section, we can now restate
Theorem

4
in a more “palatable” way by translating statements about the TV distance between distributions as quantities of their corresponding operators. More concretely we have the following:

Claim 70
.

For
X\in{\mathscr{D}}
,
d_{\mathrm{TV}}((\mathcal{D}_{1})_{X},(\mathcal{D}_{2})_{X})=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}-T_{\mathcal{D}_{2}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

Proof.

We directly compute:

\displaystyle d_{\textrm{TV}}((\mathcal{D}_{1})_{X},(\mathcal{D}_{2})_{X})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}Y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}Y]\right|}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{T_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}e_{Y}}}\right\rangle-\left\langle{{e_{X}},{T_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}-T_{\mathcal{D}_{2}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}.

Here we used
Fact

66
and that under a permutation elements in
{\mathscr{D}}
only map to
{\mathscr{D}}
.
∎

We apply this to prove
Theorem

4
:

Theorem 71

(
Theorem

4
restated)
.

Given
k\leq 2^{\sqrt{n}/500}
, then for any
t\geq 500{\left(k\log k+\frac{\log(1/\varepsilon)}{\sqrt{n}}\right)}
, the following holds. Let
\mathcal{P}^{t}
be the distribution on
\mathcal{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
defined from our circuit model with a circuit family computing an
\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}\cdot(2t+1)}
-approximate
k
-wise independent distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}}
as the base. Then
\mathcal{P}^{t}
is
\varepsilon
-approximate
k
-wise independent. That is, for all
X\in{\mathscr{D}}
we have
d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{P}^{t}_{X},\mathcal{G}_{X}\right)}\leq\varepsilon
when
n
is large enough.

Proof.

We will bound the two quantities arising from the following application of the triangle inequality:

d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{P}^{t}_{X},\mathcal{G}_{X}\right)}\leq d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{P}^{t}_{X},\mathcal{G}^{t}_{X}\right)}+d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{G}^{t}_{X},\mathcal{G}_{X}\right)}.

Here we introduce the intermediate distribution
\mathcal{G}^{t}
(defined in
Section

7.1.3
) which will facilitate our analysis. The two steps are then to bound each of the latter terms separately by
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
.

Lemma 72
.

Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem

4
. Then for any
X\in{\mathscr{D}}
:

\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{P}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{P}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}})f}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}

where
\mathcal{F}
is the set of functions
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{nk}\to[-1,1]
with
\mathrm{Supp}(f)\subseteq{\mathscr{D}}
.

This lemma is a just a quantitative result capturing the intuition that if we replace all of the black-box
1
D pseudorandom permutation circuits in our design with truly random permutations, the TV distance does not change much.

Lemma 73
.

Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem

4
. Then for any
X\in{\mathscr{D}}
:

\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

This lemma claims that random columns and random row permutations approximate truly random permutations in low depth
t=\mathrm{poly}(k)
. In light of
Claim

70
, we have shown that

d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{P}^{t}_{X},\mathcal{G}^{t}_{X}\right)}+d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{G}^{t}_{X},\mathcal{G}_{X}\right)}\leq\varepsilon,

which finishes the proof of
Theorem

71
, given the two lemmas.
∎

7.4
Proof of
Lemma

72
