In this proof, we roughly want to decompose the operators of our circuit as 1) sequential pieces corresponding to each layer of the circuit and 2) for each layer, since the pieces act independently on either the columns or rows, we can represent them as tensor products of simpler operators acting only on one row or column. Since these individual pieces are assumed to be approximately
k
-wise independent, we just show that this is preserved through tensorization and sequential application.

Recall that
T_{\mathcal{P}^{t}}=T_{\mathcal{P}_{R}}{\left(T_{\mathcal{P}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{P}_{R}}\right)}^{t}
and
T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}=T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}{\left(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}\right)}^{t}
, that is, they are products of
2t-1
operators corresponding to the sequential pieces in the circuit. We can then rewrite the difference of the two operators as a telescoping sum following
Fact

69
. For clarity let
T_{\mathcal{P}^{(i)}}
denote the
i
th operator in the product
T_{\mathcal{P}^{t}}
and likewise for
T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}
.

T_{\mathcal{P}_{R}}{\left(T_{\mathcal{P}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{P}_{R}}\right)}^{t}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}{\left(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}\right)}^{t}=\sum_{i=1}^{2t+1}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}T_{\mathcal{P}^{(j)}}\cdot{\left(T_{\mathcal{P}^{(i)}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{(i)}}\right)}\cdot\prod_{j=i+1}^{s}T_{\mathcal{G}^{(j)}}

To simplify this sum, we will make use of the following claim:

Claim 74
.

Let
\mathcal{D}_{1},\mathcal{D}_{2}
be distributions on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{n}}
and
A
an operator on the space
\mathbb{R}^{\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}
. Let
X\in{\mathscr{D}}
and
f\in\mathcal{F}
. Then there exists some
X^{*}\in{\mathscr{D}}
and
f^{*}\in\mathcal{F}
s.t.:

{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{T_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}AT_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}f}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X^{*}}},{Af^{*}}}\right\rangle\right|}.

Proof.

Observe:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{T_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}AT_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}f}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle={\left|T_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}AT_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}f(X)\right|}

\displaystyle={\left|\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}Y]\cdot AT_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}f(Y)\right|}

\displaystyle={\left|\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{D}_{1}}}Y]\cdot\left\langle{{e_{Y}},{AT_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}f}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{X^{*}\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X^{*}}},{AT_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}f}}\right\rangle\right|}.

In the last line we are using triangle inequality. For the second part of the proof, it suffices to claim
T_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}f\in\mathcal{F}
. To see this just note:

T_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}f(Z)=\sum_{Y\in\mathrm{Supp}(\mathcal{D}_{2}(Z)))}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[Z\to_{T_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}}Y]\cdot f(Y)

Since
f
maps to
[-1,1]
which is a convex set,
T_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}f
maps to
[-1,1]
as well. Additionally, for any
Z\notin{\mathscr{D}}
, the support of
(\mathcal{D}_{2})_{Z}
cannot intersect
{\mathscr{D}}
so the term is 0, thus
T_{\mathcal{D}_{2}}f
is supported on a subset of
{\mathscr{D}}
so is in
\mathcal{F}
. ∎

Claim 75
.

\max_{X\in{\mathscr{D}},f\in\mathcal{F}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{{\left(T_{\mathcal{P}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}\right)}f}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq{\left(2t+1\right)}\cdot\max_{X\in{\mathscr{D}},f\in\mathcal{F}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{{\left(T_{\mathcal{P}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}\right)}f}}\right\rangle\right|}.

Noting importantly that the latter term is interchangeable for
T_{\mathcal{P}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}
and
T_{\mathcal{P}_{C}}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}
.

Proof.

We directly compute:

\displaystyle\max_{X\in{\mathscr{D}},f\in\mathcal{F}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{{\left(T_{\mathcal{P}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}\right)}f}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=\max_{X\in{\mathscr{D}},f\in\mathcal{F}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{{\left(\sum_{i=1}^{2t+1}\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}T_{\mathcal{P}^{(j)}}\cdot{\left(T_{\mathcal{P}^{(i)}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{(i)}}\right)}\cdot\prod_{j=i+1}^{s}T_{\mathcal{G}^{(j)}}\right)}f}}\right\rangle\right|}

(
Fact

69
)

\displaystyle\leq\max_{X\in{\mathscr{D}},f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{2t+1}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{{\left(\prod_{j=1}^{i-1}T_{\mathcal{P}^{(j)}}\cdot{\left(T_{\mathcal{P}^{(i)}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{(i)}}\right)}\cdot\prod_{j=i+1}^{s}T_{\mathcal{G}^{(j)}}\right)}f}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{X\in{\mathscr{D}},f\in\mathcal{F}}\sum_{i=1}^{2t+1}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{{\left(T_{\mathcal{P}^{(i)}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{(i)}}\right)}f}}\right\rangle\right|}

(
Claim

74
)

\displaystyle\leq(2t+1)\cdot\max_{X\in{\mathscr{D}},f\in\mathcal{F}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{{\left(T_{\mathcal{P}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}\right)}f}}\right\rangle\right|}.

In the last line we are using that
T_{\mathcal{P}_{R}}
and
T_{\mathcal{P}_{C}}
are symmetric, and likewise for
T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}
and
T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}
.
∎

It is worth pointing out at this point we could convert back to the total variation distance making the above statement:

d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{P}^{t}_{X},\mathcal{G}^{t}_{X}\right)}\leq(2t+1)\cdot d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left((\mathcal{P}_{R})_{X},(\mathcal{G}_{R})_{X}\right)}.

We have in essence reduced the TV distance bound on our sequential circuit to just a single layer. Our next move will be to reduce the distance further to the individual parallel gates making up each layer, which is what we assumed black box is
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-approximate
k
-wise independent. Towards this end we write:

\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{R}}}Y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Y]\right|}=\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\prod_{i=1}^{\sqrt{n}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]-\prod_{i=1}^{\sqrt{n}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]\right|}.

We denote here
\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}=\mathcal{U}(\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}})
. The key fact here is that the operators correspond to product distributions on individual rows. We can again utilize
Fact

69
to simplify the difference of products:

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\sum_{j=1}^{\sqrt{n}}\prod_{i=1}^{j-1}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]\cdot{\left(\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}Y_{j,\cdot}]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}Y_{j,\cdot}]\right)}\cdot\prod_{i=j+1}^{\sqrt{n}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\prod_{i=1}^{j-1}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]\cdot{\left(\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}Y_{j,\cdot}]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}Y_{j,\cdot}]\right)}\cdot\prod_{i=j+1}^{\sqrt{n}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{y\in\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}y]\right|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\in{\mathscr{D}}\\
Y_{j,\cdot}=y\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{j-1}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]\prod_{i=j+1}^{\sqrt{n}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{y\in\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}y]\right|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}\\
Y_{j,\cdot}=y\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{j-1}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]\prod_{i=j+1}^{\sqrt{n}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{y\in\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}y]\right|}.

Here we are partitioning the sum over
Y
into its fixed row
Y_{j,\cdot}
. The large sum of products we get is just the probability the “free” rows map to different elements of
\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}
, which marginalizes to 1 when we sum over the entire region. We are nearly done, as the term now looks very close to that which shows up in the definition of
\varepsilon
-approximate
k
-wise independent. The only difference is that we sum over the entire set of rows
\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}
, whereas in the definition of
\varepsilon
-approximate
k
-wise independence, the sum is over “distinct”
k
-tuples. Distinct
k
-tuples of “grids”
X
may share rows
X_{j,\cdot}
that are not distinctly colored. Nonetheless, our result still follows from the definition of
\varepsilon
-approximate
k
-wise independence.

Using
Lemma

76
below, we compute

\max_{f\in\mathcal{F}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{P}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}})f}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq(2t+1)\cdot\sum_{j=1}^{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\varepsilon}{2\sqrt{n}\cdot(2t+1)}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

This completes the proof of
Lemma

72
.

Lemma 76
.

For every
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}
we have:

\sum_{y\in\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}y]\right|}\leq\frac{2\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}\cdot(2t+1)}.

Proof of
Lemma

76
.

For
x
corresponding to a distinct
k
-tuple, this reduces to the term in
\varepsilon^{\prime}
-approximate
k
-wise independence, for which
\mathcal{B}
is assumed to fulfill. The proof is then a matter of showing that “
\varepsilon
-approximate
k
-wise independence” implies “
\varepsilon
-approximate
\tau
-wise independence” for
\tau<k
. For this, we will need a notion of color class for elements in
\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}
analogous to the one defined in
Section

7.1.2
. We will define for an equivalence relation
R
on
[k]
:

B_{R}=\left\{x\in\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}\mid x^{i}=x^{j}\iff iRj\right\}.

That is, we think of
x
as a
k
-tuple of rows and take the corresponding coloring.

First note that we only need to consider terms such that
y\in B(x)
, as if they are not colored the same then the transition probability under any permutation becomes 0. Now assume
x
is
\tau
-colored for
\tau<k
and
B=B(x)
. Let
T
be the set of indices corresponding to the first instances of a color appearing in
x
. For example, if
x
was colored with
k-1
colors, with the first and last elements of the
k
-tuple colored the same, then
T
would be
[k-1]
. Importantly,
T
is the same across the color class
B(x)
and
{\left|T\right|}=\tau
. We will then create a function
\varphi_{B}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}k}\to\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}\tau}
that projects out the indices outside of
T
. As a result we have for all
y\in B
,
\varphi_{B}(y)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\sqrt{n}}^{(\tau)}
, the set of distinct tuples in
\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}\tau}
and moreover the image of
\varphi_{B}
under
B
is entirely
{\mathscr{D}}_{\sqrt{n}}^{(\tau)}
. The key observation is then that
[k]\setminus T
, the indices not in
T
, can be ignored across transitions since they are completely fixed:

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in B(x)}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}y]\right|}=

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in B}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\varphi_{B}(x)\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}\varphi_{B}(y)]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\varphi_{B}(x)\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}\varphi_{B}(y)]\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi_{B}(y)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\sqrt{n}}^{(\tau)}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\varphi_{B}(x)\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}\varphi_{B}(y)]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\varphi_{B}(x)\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}\varphi_{B}(y)]\right|}.

Note the end formula above has no dependence on the fixed indices
[k]\setminus T
. This allows us to pretend they are distinct, writing the above sum over elements of
{\mathscr{D}}
instead:

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in B(x)}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}y]\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi_{B}(y)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\sqrt{n}}^{(\tau)}}{\left|\sum_{y_{[k]\setminus T}\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\sqrt{n}}^{(k-\tau)}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[(\varphi_{B}(x),\cdot)\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),y_{[k]\setminus T})]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\varphi_{B}(x)\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),y_{[k]\setminus T})]\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi_{B}(y)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\sqrt{n}}^{(\tau)}\\
y_{[k]\setminus T}\in{\mathscr{D}}_{\sqrt{n}}^{(k-\tau)}\end{subarray}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[(\varphi_{B}(x),\cdot)\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),y_{[k]\setminus T})]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\varphi_{B}(x)\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),y_{[k]\setminus T})]\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in{\mathscr{D}}^{(k)}_{\sqrt{n}}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[(\varphi_{B}(x),\cdot)\to_{T_{\mathcal{B}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),y_{[k]\setminus T})]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\varphi_{B}(x)\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{\sqrt{n}}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),y_{[k]\setminus T})]\right|}.

In this last line we can choose
(\varphi_{B}(x),\cdot)
to be from
{\mathscr{D}}^{(k)}_{\sqrt{n}}
so any
y
outside of this class contributes nothing to the sum. Appealing to the approximate
k
-wise independence of
\mathcal{B}
finishes the proof.
∎

7.5
Proof of
Lemma

73

The following lemma will help us achieve the bound in
Lemma

73
.

Lemma 77
.

Assume the hypothesis of
Lemma

73
. Then for any
Y\in{\mathscr{D}}
, we have
{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{t+1}{2^{\sqrt{n}(t-1)/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}
.

To see why the lemma is sufficient, observe:

\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{t+1}{2^{\sqrt{n}(t-1)/128}}\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}\leq\frac{t+1}{2^{\sqrt{n}(t-1)/128}}\sum_{B\in{\mathscr{B}}}\sum_{Y\in B}\frac{1}{{\left|B\right|}}\leq\frac{{\left|{\mathscr{B}}\right|}\cdot(t+1)}{2^{\sqrt{n}(t-1)/128}}.

Here we partition the sum based on color classes, and note that each color class contributes a total of 1 to the sum. We can use
Fact

65
bounding the number of color classes and the fact that
\frac{t+1}{2^{t-1}}
very quickly to write:

\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{k^{k\sqrt{n}}\cdot(t+1)}{2^{\sqrt{n}(t-1)/128}}\leq\frac{k^{k\sqrt{n}}}{2^{{\left(\sqrt{n}/128-1\right)}(t-1)}}\leq\frac{\varepsilon}{2}.

This is then bounded by
\frac{\varepsilon}{2}
for
t\geq\frac{\sqrt{n}k\log_{2}k+\log_{2}2/\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}/128-1}+1
. When
n
is large enough, the bound holds when
t\geq 500{\left(k\log_{2}k+\frac{\log_{2}1/\varepsilon}{\sqrt{n}}\right)}
.

Proof of
Lemma

77
.

Recall that
T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}=T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}})^{t}
. We can then write
T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}}=(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})
and prove the claim by induction on
t
. Consider first when
t=0
.

{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}={\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}}}Y]\right|}={\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[Y\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}X]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[Y\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}}}X]\right|}.

We use the self-adjointness of the two operators here. Observe that under the action of
T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}
,
Y
goes to a uniform element of
B(Y)
, and under
T_{\mathcal{G}}
goes to a uniform element of
{\mathscr{D}}
. Thus, the quantity is either
\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}-\frac{1}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}
or
\frac{1}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}
. Either way it is below
\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}
. For the induction step, we assume the lemma for fixed
t\geq 0
. Then we compute

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t+1}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle={\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle={\left|T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}{\left(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}\right)}(X)\right|}

\displaystyle={\left|\sum_{Z\in{\mathscr{D}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Z]{\left(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}\right)}(Z)\right|}

\displaystyle={\left|\sum_{Z\in{\mathscr{D}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Z]{\left((T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}\right)}(Z)\right|}

\displaystyle={\left|\sum_{Z\in{\mathscr{D}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Z]\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}.

We have managed to write the
(t+1)
case inner product as a convex combination of the case with
t
. However, if we try to apply the induction hypothesis here we will make no progress. Instead, we will break the sum up and handle only one half with induction. The other half we will bound “from scratch”, and it is here we will make progress. Recall that we may partition
{\mathscr{D}}
into two regions,
B_{\text{safe}}
and
B_{\text{coll}}
:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t+1}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\sum_{Z\in B_{\text{safe}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Z]\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\;\;+{\left|\sum_{Z\in B_{\text{coll}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Z]\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\sum_{Z\in B_{\text{safe}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Z]\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\;\;+\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}B_{\text{coll}}]\cdot\frac{t+1}{2^{\sqrt{n}(t-1)/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}.

In the last line we used the inductive hypothesis. We will then show that the first term is smaller than is demanded by the induction due to a straightforward spectral norm argument. The second term is small because the probability of “collision”, or that a walk transitions to
B_{\mathrm{coll}}
, is small. More specifically we will need the following two lemmas which we will prove in
Section

8
.

Lemma 78
.

Assuming
k\leq 2^{\sqrt{n}/500}
and
n
large enough,
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}/128}}
.

Lemma 79
.

Assuming
k\leq 2^{\sqrt{n}/500}
, for all
X\in{\mathscr{D}}
,
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}B_{\text{coll}}]\leq\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}/128}}
.

To use
Lemma

78
we write for
Z\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}
:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}=

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})^{t}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}}\right\rVert}{2}^{t}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}e_{Z}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}e_{Y}\right\rVert}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}t/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}^{1/2}{\left|B_{\text{safe}}\right|}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}t/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}.

The first step uses the self-adjointness of
T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}
, the fact that
T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}^{2}=T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}
, and
Fact

68
. The inequality is an application of Cauchy-Schwarz and submultiplicativity of the operator norm. The second to last step uses
Lemma

78
and
Claim

80
below, and the last step uses
Fact

66
, namely that
B_{\mathrm{safe}}
is larger than every other color class given our choice of
k
and large enough
n
.

Claim 80
.

For arbitrary
U\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
:

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}e_{U}\right\rVert}_{2}=\frac{1}{{\left|B(U)\right|}^{1/2}}.

Continuing from the equation above we have:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t+1}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\sum_{Z\in B_{\text{safe}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Z]\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\;\;+\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}B_{\text{coll}}]\cdot\frac{t+1}{2^{\sqrt{n}(t-1)/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}t/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}

(
Lemma

78
)

\displaystyle\;\;+\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}B_{\text{coll}}]\cdot\frac{t+1}{2^{\sqrt{n}(t-1)/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}t/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}+\frac{t+1}{2^{\sqrt{n}t/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}

(
Lemma

79
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{t+2}{2^{\sqrt{n}t/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}.

This completes the induction. We finish by proving the claim above:

Proof of
Claim

80
.

Observe:

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}e_{U}\right\rVert}_{2}=\sqrt{\sum_{W\in B(U)}{\left(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}e_{U}(W)\right)}^{2}}=\sqrt{\sum_{W\in B(U)}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[W\to_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}U]^{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{W\in B(U)}{\left(\frac{1}{{\left|B(U)\right|}}\right)}^{2}}=\frac{1}{{\left|B(U)\right|}^{1/2}}.\qed

This completes the proof of
Lemma

77
.
∎

8
Proof of Spectral Properties of
T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}
and
T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}

In this section we prove
Lemma

78
, which is a spectral norm bound on the difference between two operators related to our constructions above. As an intermediate result in the proof we will show
Lemma

79
as well. This will then conclude the proof of
Theorem

4
.

Lemma 81

(
Lemma

78
restated)
.

Assuming
k\leq 2^{\sqrt{n}/500}
, we have for large enough
n
,

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}/128}},

or rather for
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{nk}\to\mathbb{R}
:

\left\langle{{f},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f}}\right\rangle\leq\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}/128}}\cdot\left\langle{{f},{f}}\right\rangle.

Note that it suffices to prove maximization across symmetric linear forms because the operator is self-adjoint. We will proceed by decomposing
f=f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}+f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}+f_{B_{=0}}
where
f_{S}
is supported on
S\subseteq\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
. Note that these regions form a partition of
\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
, so these functions are orthogonal to one another.

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle+{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{\mathscr{D}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle+{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{=0}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{B_{=0}}}}\right\rangle\right|}.

Note that the cross terms involving
B_{=0}
are all zero, as a permutation will not cross between these regions. Our proof will bound each of these terms separately.

8.1
f
Supported on
B_{\textrm{safe}}

Lemma 82
.

{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{4\sqrt{n}k^{2}}{2^{\sqrt{n}}}\cdot\left\langle{{f},{f}}\right\rangle
.

Proof.

Let
X\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}
,
g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{nk}\to\mathbb{R}
.

\displaystyle(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})g(X)

\displaystyle=\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}Y]\cdot g(Y)-\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}}}Y]\cdot g(Y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\left|B_{\mathrm{safe}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}g(Y)-\frac{1}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}g(Y)

\displaystyle={\left(\frac{1}{{\left|B_{\mathrm{safe}}\right|}}-\frac{1}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}\right)}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}g(Y)-\frac{1}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{coll}}}g(Y)

\displaystyle={\left(1-\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{safe}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B_{\mathrm{safe}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}g(Y)-\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{coll}}}g(Y)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}{\left(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-\mathcal{H}\right)}g(X).

Our definition of
\mathcal{H}g(X)=\frac{1}{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{coll}}}g(Y)
corresponds to the random walk operator that puts all probability weight into
B_{\mathrm{coll}}
uniformly. Note that
\mathcal{H}
does not correspond to any random walk induced by a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}
(so it cannot be written as
T_{\mathcal{H}}
), but is still a random walk operator. With this in hand we may write:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{X\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}(X)\cdot(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}f_{{\mathscr{D}}})(X)

\displaystyle=\sum_{X\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}(X)\cdot(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}f_{{\mathscr{D}}})(X)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}\sum_{X\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}(X)\cdot(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-\mathcal{H})(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}f_{{\mathscr{D}}})(X)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-\mathcal{H}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}.

The only important fact about
\mathcal{H}
is that it is a valid random walk operator, which allows us to use
Lemma

23
to bound this final inner product crudely as:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}{\left({\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}f_{{\mathscr{D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{\mathcal{H}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{\mathscr{D}}\right\rVert}_{2}

(
Lemma

23
)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}\langle f,f\rangle.

Fact

66
then suffices to prove the claim. ∎

8.2
f
Supported on
B_{\textrm{coll}}

Lemma 83
.

{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{\mathscr{D}}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{8\sqrt{n}k^{2}}{2^{\sqrt{n}/32}}\left\langle{{f},{f}}\right\rangle
.

Proof.

First, we can decompose
f_{\mathscr{D}}=f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}+f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}
and bound:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{\mathscr{D}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}.

By the self-adjointness of the operator, the first term is bounded by the case above, so it suffices to bound the latter. For this term, we can appeal directly to
Lemma

23
and the triangle inequality to get:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\max_{X\in B_{\mathrm{coll}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}B_{\mathrm{coll}}]+\max_{X\in B_{\mathrm{coll}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}}}B_{\mathrm{coll}}]}\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}}}\right\rangle.

Note that regardless of choice of
X
, the latter probability
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}}}B_{\mathrm{coll}}]=\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}\right|}}
which is less than
\frac{2\sqrt{n}k^{2}}{2^{\sqrt{n}}}
by
Fact

66
. For the former, we will need a slightly more detailed analysis which also serves as the proof of
Lemma

79
in the previous section:

Lemma 84

(Restatement of
Lemma

79
)
.

For all
X\in{\mathscr{D}}
,
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}B_{\mathrm{coll}}]\leq\frac{2\sqrt{n}k^{2}}{2^{\sqrt{n}/16}}
.

The lemma is immediately sufficient to achieve the bound in
Lemma

83
.
∎

Proof of
Lemma

84
.

We will apply a union bound over the probability of any pair of rows “colliding”, which would put them in
B_{\mathrm{coll}}
. Let
X\in{\mathscr{D}}
. We will model our process as:

X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}}\bm{Y}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}}\bm{Z}.

We then fix
\bm{Z}^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}
and
\bm{Z}^{m}_{i,\cdot}
for
i\in[\sqrt{n}]
,
\ell\neq m\in[k]
. The only fact we will use about
\bm{Y}
is that for some
j\in[k]
(potentially equal to
i
), we have
(\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},\bm{Y}^{m}_{j,\cdot})
are uniform from
{\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}\choose 2}
. To see this note that there must exist some
j
s.t.
X^{\ell}_{j,\cdot}\neq X^{m}_{j,\cdot}
, otherwise
X\notin{\mathscr{D}}
. Since the permutation applied to these two rows is uniform from
\mathfrak{S}_{\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}}
, the resulting rows in
Y
look like a uniform unequal pair.

With this in mind, we will now condition on the event that
d(\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},\bm{Y}^{m}_{j,\cdot})\geq\sqrt{n}/4
(distance here is Hamming distance), allowing us to split our analysis into two cases:

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\bm{Z}^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}=\bm{Z}_{i,\cdot}^{m}]\leq

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\bm{Z}^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}=\bm{Z}^{m}_{i,\cdot}\mid d(\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},\bm{Y}^{m}_{j,\cdot})>\sqrt{n}/4]+\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[d(\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},\bm{Y}^{m}_{j,\cdot})\leq\sqrt{n}/4]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}/4}}+\frac{1}{e^{\sqrt{n}/16}}

(
Lemma

85
,
Lemma

86
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2}{2^{\sqrt{n}/16}}.

Applying a union bound over
\leq\sqrt{n}k^{2}
rows completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 85
.

\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\bm{Z}^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}=\bm{Z}^{m}_{i,\cdot}\mid d(\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},\bm{Y}^{m}_{j,\cdot})>\sqrt{n}/4]\leq\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}/4}}
.

Proof.

The probability that
\bm{Z}^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}
and
\bm{Z}^{m}_{i,\cdot}
are equal can be viewed as the probability that all of their individual bits are equal, and they are all independent since they come from independently sampled column permutations. Since
\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{j,\cdot}
and
\bm{Y}^{m}_{j,\cdot}
differ in at least
\sqrt{n}/4
places,
\bm{Y}^{\ell}
and
\bm{Y}^{m}
must differ in at least that many columns. In these columns, it can be seen that the corresponding bits in
\bm{Z}^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}
and
Z^{m}_{i,\cdot}
are the same with probability
\leq\frac{1}{2}
(the probability is exactly one half when the columns are sampled uniformly independently, conditioning that they are unequal only lowers this probability). By independence the probability is less than
\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}/4}}
. ∎

Lemma 86
.

\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[d(\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},\bm{Y}^{m}_{j,\cdot})\leq\sqrt{n}/4]\leq\frac{1}{e^{\sqrt{n}/16}}
.

Proof.

Note that
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[d(\bm{Y}^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},\bm{Y}^{m}_{j,\cdot})\leq\sqrt{n}/4]\leq\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\bm{x},\bm{y}\sim\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}}[d(\bm{x},\bm{y})\leq\sqrt{n}/4]
. For uniform
x,y
, the random variable
d(x,y)
is the sum of
\sqrt{n}
independent Bernoulli
(1/2)
random variables. This has expectation
\sqrt{n}/2
and thus by Hoeffding’s Inequality:

\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\bm{x},\bm{y}\sim\{\pm 1\}^{\sqrt{n}}}[d(\bm{x},\bm{y})\leq\sqrt{n}/4]\leq e^{-\sqrt{n}/16}.\qed

8.3
The Induction Case

Lemma 87
.

Let
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{nk}\to\mathbb{R}
be supported on
B_{=0}
for
k\geq 2
. Then, we have

{\left|\left\langle{{f},{{\left(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}^{(k)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}^{(k)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}^{(k)}-T_{\mathcal{G}}^{(k)}\right)}f}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}^{(k-1)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}^{(k-1)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}^{(k-1)}-T_{\mathcal{G}}^{(k-1)}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}\left\langle{{f},{f}}\right\rangle.

Proof.

The proof follows from the same proof as the proof of
Lemma

40
.
∎

8.4
Wrapping Up

Putting together all three cases we have:

{\left|\left\langle{{f},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{4\sqrt{n}k^{2}}{2^{\sqrt{n}}}\left\langle{{f},{f}}\right\rangle+\frac{8\sqrt{n}k^{2}}{2^{\sqrt{n}/32}}\left\langle{{f},{f}}\right\rangle+\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}^{(k-1)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}^{(k-1)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}^{(k-1)}-T_{\mathcal{G}}^{(k-1)}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}\left\langle{{f},{f}}\right\rangle.

Since
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}^{(1)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}^{(1)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}^{(1)}-T_{\mathcal{G}}^{(1)}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}=0
and by assumption
k\leq 2^{\sqrt{n}/500}
, it follows by induction that:

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}^{(k)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}^{(k)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{R}}^{(k)}-T_{\mathcal{G}}^{(k)}\right\rVert}_{\mathrm{op}}\leq\sum_{\ell=2}^{k}{\left(\frac{4\sqrt{n}\ell^{2}}{2^{\sqrt{n}}}+\frac{8\sqrt{n}\ell^{2}}{2^{\sqrt{n}/32}}\right)}\leq\frac{k^{3}}{2^{\sqrt{n}/64}}\leq\frac{1}{2^{\sqrt{n}/128}}.

Appendix A
Comparison Method

Theorem 88

(
[
WLP09
]
, Theorem 13.23)
.

Let
\widetilde{P}
and
P
be transition matrices for two ergodic Markov chains on the same state space
V
. Assume that for each
(x,y)\in V^{2}
there exists a random path

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}(x,y)={\left((x,\bm{u}_{1}),(\bm{u}_{1},\bm{u}_{2}),(\bm{u}_{2},\bm{u}_{3}),\dots,(\bm{u}_{\ell},y)\right)}.

Then we have that

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}(L)\geq{\left(\max_{v\in V}\frac{\pi(v)}{\widetilde{\pi}(v)}\right)}A(\bm{\Delta})\lambda_{2}(\widetilde{L}).

where the comparison constant of
\bm{\Delta}
is defined to be

\displaystyle A(\bm{\Delta})\coloneq\max_{\begin{subarray}{c}(a,b)\in V^{2}\\
\widetilde{P}(a,b)>0\end{subarray}}{\left\{\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}(x)\widetilde{P}(a,b)}\sum_{(x,y)\in V^{2}}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\bm{\Delta}}{\left[\mathbf{1}_{(a,b)\in\bm{\Delta}(x,y)}\cdot|\bm{\Delta}(x,y)|\right]}\cdot\pi(x)\cdot P(x,y)\right\}}.

Here
\pi
and
\widetilde{\pi}
are the (unique) stationary distributions for
P
and
\widetilde{P}
, respectively, and
\mathbf{1}_{(a,b)\in\mathcal{P}}
is the indicator variable which captures whether
(a,b)
appears in the sequence
\mathcal{P}
.

Lemma

32
is a direct consequence of the following
Corollary

89
, with symmetry applied.

Corollary 89
.

Let
\widetilde{P}
and
P
be transition matrices for random walks on undirected (multi)graphs
\widetilde{G}=(V,\widetilde{E})
and
G=(V,E)
, respectively. The graph
G
is
d
-regular, and
\widetilde{G}
is
\widetilde{d}
-regular. Assume that for each
e\in E
there exists a random path

\displaystyle\bm{\Gamma}(e)=(\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{T_{e}}),

where
(\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{1},\dots,\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{T_{e}})
9
9

9

Here
T_{e}
is a (deterministic) quantity determined by the edge
e
.
is drawn from a distribution on sequences of edges connecting the endpoints of
E
. Then we have for any
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}V\to\mathbb{R}
that

\displaystyle\lambda_{2}(L)\geq{\left(\max_{v\in V}\frac{\pi(v)}{\widetilde{\pi}(v)}\right)}B(\bm{\Gamma})\lambda_{2}(\widetilde{L}).

where the
multigraph comparison constant
is defined as the maximum congestion over all edges
\tilde{e}\in\widetilde{E}
connecting vertices
a,b\in V
,

\displaystyle B(\bm{\Gamma})\coloneq\max_{\widetilde{e}\in\widetilde{E}}{\left\{\frac{\widetilde{d}}{d}\sum_{e\in E}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\bm{\Gamma}}{\left[\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{e}\in\bm{\Gamma}(e)}\cdot|\bm{\Gamma}(e)|\right]}\right\}}.

Here
\pi
and
\widetilde{\pi}
are the (unique) stationary distributions for
P
and
\widetilde{P}
, respectively.

Proof.

We construct a (randomized) map
\bm{\Delta}
as in
Lemma

32
from the map
\bm{\Gamma}
as follows. For each
(x,y)\in V^{2}
, if
x
and
y
are not connected by an edge in
E
then set
\bm{\Delta}(x,y)=()
(the sequence of length 0). Otherwise select a random edge
\bm{e}
from
(E)_{x,y}
and let
{\left(\bm{\widetilde{e}}_{1},\dots,\bm{\widetilde{e}}_{{T_{e}}}\right)}
be the random path
\bm{\Gamma}(\bm{e})
. For each
i\in[T_{e}]
let
(\bm{u}_{i},\bm{v}_{i})
be the vertices connected by
\widetilde{\bm{e}}_{i}
. Then set

\displaystyle\bm{\Delta}(x,y)={\left((\bm{u}_{1},\bm{v}_{1}),\dots,(\bm{u}_{\ell},\bm{v}_{T_{e}})\right)}={\left((x,\bm{v}_{1}),\dots,(\bm{u}_{T_{e}},y)\right)}.

The comparison constant of
\bm{\Delta}
is

A(\bm{\Delta})=\max_{(a,b)\in V^{2}}{\left\{\frac{1}{\widetilde{\pi}(x)\widetilde{P}(a,b)}\sum_{(x,y)\in V^{2}}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\bm{\Delta}}{\left[\mathbf{1}_{(a,b)\in\bm{\Delta}(x,y)}\cdot|\bm{\Delta}(x,y)|\right]}\cdot\pi_{\mathsf{ref}}(x)\cdot P(x,y)\right\}}.

Since both Markov chains have the same stationary distribution over the same state space, the
\pi
terms cancel, so the above is equal to

\displaystyle\max_{(a,b)\in V^{2}}{\left\{\frac{1}{\widetilde{P}(a,b)}\sum_{(x,y)\in V^{2}}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\bm{\Delta}}{\left[\mathbf{1}_{(a,b)\in\bm{\Delta}(x,y)}\cdot|\bm{\Delta}(x,y)|\right]}\cdot P(x,y)\right\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{(a,b)\in V^{2}}{\left\{\frac{\widetilde{d}}{{\left|\widetilde{E}_{a,b}\right|}}\sum_{(x,y)\in V^{2}}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\bm{\Delta}}{\left[\mathbf{1}_{(a,b)\in\bm{\Delta}(x,y)}\cdot|\bm{\Delta}(x,y)|\right]}\cdot\frac{{\left|E_{x,y}\right|}}{d}\right\}}.

Let us now start translating from pairs of vertices to edges of the multigraph. Each pair of vertices
(x,y)
has
\left|E_{x,y}\right|
edges connecting them, we can change the summation from pairs of vertices to edges
e\in E
. Recall that
u(e),v(e)
are the endpoints of edge
e
. Continuing the calculation, the above is equal to

\displaystyle\max_{(a,b)\in V^{2}}{\left\{\frac{\widetilde{d}}{d{\left|\widetilde{E}_{a,b}\right|}}\sum_{e\in E}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\bm{\Delta}}{\left[\mathbf{1}_{(a,b)\in\bm{\Delta}(u(e),v(e))}\cdot|\bm{\Delta}(u(e),v(e))|\right]}\right\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{(a,b)\in V^{2}}{\left\{\frac{\widetilde{d}}{d}\sum_{e\in E}\frac{1}{{\left|\widetilde{E}_{a,b}\right|}}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\bm{\Delta}}{\left[\mathbf{1}_{(a,b)\in\bm{\Delta}(u(e),v(e))}\cdot|\bm{\Delta}(u(e),v(e))|\right]}\right\}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{(a,b)\in V^{2}}{\left\{\frac{\widetilde{d}}{d}\sum_{e\in E}\frac{1}{{\left|\widetilde{E}_{a,b}\right|}}|\bm{\Gamma}(e)|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{\bm{\Delta}}{\left[\mathbf{1}_{(a,b)\in\bm{\Delta}(u(e),v(e))}\right]}\right\}}.

The last equality is because
|\bm{\Delta}(u(e),v(e))|=|\bm{\Gamma}(e)|
with certainty, and
|\bm{\Gamma}(e)|
is a deterministic quantity that only depends on
e
.

The sum of probabilities that
\widetilde{e}\in(\widetilde{E})_{a,b}
appears in the sequence
\bm{\Gamma}(e)
, over all such
\widetilde{e}
, is equal to the probability that
(a,b)
appears in
\bm{\Delta}(u(e),v(e))
. By averaging, we have that the probability that
\widetilde{e}\in\widetilde{E}_{a,b}
appears in
\bm{\Gamma}(e)
, where
\widetilde{e}
maximizes this quantity, is at least
\frac{1}{|\widetilde{E}_{a,b}|}
times the appearance probability of
(a,b)
. This results in

\displaystyle A(\bm{\Delta})\leq

\displaystyle\max_{\widetilde{e}\in\widetilde{E}}{\left\{\frac{\widetilde{d}}{d}\sum_{e\in E}\mathop{{\bf E}\/}_{\bm{\Gamma}}{\left[\mathbf{1}_{\widetilde{e}\in\bm{\Gamma}(e)}\cdot|\bm{\Gamma}(e)|\right]}\right\}}=B(\bm{\Gamma}).

Applying
Theorem

88
with this new map
\bm{\Delta}
completes the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Extension to
D
-dimensional Lattices

B.1
More Bit Arrays and Color Classes

For
1\leq D^{\prime}\leq D
, we regard an element
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}
as a function
x\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}^{\otimes D^{\prime}}\to\{\pm 1\}
.
Similarly, we regard an element
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}
as a function
X\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}^{\otimes D^{\prime}}\times{\left[k\right]}\to\{\pm 1\}
.
For
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}
,
i\in{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}
,
\tau\in{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}^{\otimes D^{\prime}-1}
and
\ell\in[k]
, we use the notation:

•

X^{\ell}_{i,\tau}=X(i,\tau,\ell)\in\{\pm 1\}

•

X^{\ell}=X\mid_{{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}^{\otimes D^{\prime}}\times\{\ell\}}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}

•

X_{i,\cdot}=X\mid_{\{i\}\times{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}^{\otimes D^{\prime}-1}\times[k]}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}k}

•

X_{\cdot,\tau}=X\mid_{{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}\times\{\tau\}\times[k]}\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{1/D}k}

Our definition for coloring will remain the same, namely for
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}
we will say
X^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}
and
X^{m}_{i,\cdot}
are colored the same if they are equal, but it is worth noting that these objects are
(D^{\prime}-1)
-dimensional sublattices and the underlying relations are then
n^{1/D}
tuples of equivalence relations. Note that in the case
D=2
these do in fact correspond to rows. Since the number of such sublattices is
n^{1/D}
in general, the number of color classes is at most
k^{kn^{1/D}}
.

Our partition into
B_{\mathrm{safe}}
,
B_{\mathrm{coll}}
, and
B_{=0}
remains mostly the same but based on the generalized notion of color class defined above:

\displaystyle B_{\text{safe}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\left\{X\in{\mathscr{D}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\forall\ell\neq m\in[k],i\in[n^{1/D}],X^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}\neq X^{m}_{i,\cdot}\right\}},

\displaystyle B_{\text{coll}}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}={\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\setminus B_{\text{safe}},

\displaystyle B_{=0}\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}=\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}\setminus{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}.

Throughout this section the value of
D^{\prime}
will be clear from context.

Fact 90
.

\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}\leq\frac{2n^{1/D}k^{2}}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}
.

Proof.

We write:

\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}=\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}\right|}}\cdot\frac{{\left|\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}.

The process of sampling from
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}
can now be seen as sampling
n^{1/D}k
sublattices from
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}
. Under this view, a simple union bound tells us that there are at most
n^{1/D}k^{2}
possible collisions, allowing us to bound the probability by
\frac{n^{1/D}k^{2}}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}
. Again, this bounds the size of
{\left|B_{=0}\right|}
as well, allowing us to crudely claim
\frac{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}{{\left|\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}\right|}}\geq\frac{1}{2}
using our assumption on
k
.
∎

B.2
Inductively Defined Random Permutations

Fix
n
,
k
, and
D\geq 2
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{1}
be a random permutation of
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{1/D}}
. We will inductively define for all
2\leq D^{\prime}\leq D
a random permutation
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}
on
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}{\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{P}_{C}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}{\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}
such that
\pi\sim\mathcal{P}_{C}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\sigma_{\tau}\sim\mathcal{P}_{1}
independently for each
\tau\in{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}^{\otimes D^{\prime}-1}
and define
\pi
such that
\pi(x)_{\cdot,\tau}=\sigma_{\tau}(x_{\cdot,\tau})
for all
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}
and all
\tau\in{\left[n^{1/D}\right]}^{\otimes D^{\prime}-1}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{P}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}{\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}
such that
\pi\sim\mathcal{P}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\sigma_{i}\sim\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}
independently for each
i\in{\left[n^{1/D}\right]}
and define
\pi
such that
\pi(x)_{i,\cdot}=\sigma_{i}(x_{i,\cdot})
for all
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}
and all
i\in{\left[n^{1/D^{\prime}}\right]}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{P}^{0}_{D^{\prime}}=\mathcal{P}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}
. For all
s\geq 1
, let
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}^{s}
be the distribution on
\mathfrak{S}{\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}
such that
\pi\sim\mathcal{P}^{s}_{D^{\prime}}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\sigma_{1}\sim\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}^{s-1}
,
\sigma_{2}\sim\mathcal{P}_{C}
, and
\sigma_{3}\sim\mathcal{P}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}
and define
\pi=\sigma_{3}\circ\sigma_{2}\circ\sigma_{1}
.

•

Set
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}=\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}^{t}
, where
t
is the constant from
Lemma

91
below if
D^{\prime}\geq 3
. Otherwise if
D^{\prime}=2
then set
t=\Theta(k\log k)
, where the constant is chosen from the statement of
Theorem

71
.

For ease of analyzing the above random permutations, we define the idealized versions of the above distributions based on the following pieces.

•

Let
\mathcal{G}_{C}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}{\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}
such that
\pi\sim\mathcal{G}_{C}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\sigma_{\tau}\sim\mathcal{U}{\left(\mathfrak{S}{\{\pm 1\}^{n^{1/D}}}\right)}
independently for each
\tau\in{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}^{\otimes D^{\prime}-1}
and define
\pi
such that
\pi(x)_{\cdot,\tau}=\sigma_{\tau}(x_{\cdot,\tau})
for all
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
and all
\tau\in{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}^{\otimes D^{\prime}-1}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{G}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}
be a distribution on
\mathfrak{S}{\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}
such that
\pi\sim\mathcal{G}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}
is sampled as follows:
Sample
\sigma_{i}\sim\mathcal{U}{\left(\mathfrak{S}{\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}\right)}
independently for each
i\in{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}
and define
\pi
such that
\pi(x)_{i,\cdot}=\sigma_{i}(x_{i,\cdot})
for all
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n}
and all
i\in{\left[n^{1/D}\,\right]}
.

B.3
Generalization of Main Theorem

Our proof will largely follow the blueprint of the
D=2
case, our main result. For
X\in{\mathscr{D}}
,

d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{P}^{t}_{D,X},\mathcal{G}_{X}\right)}\leq d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{P}^{t}_{D,X},\mathcal{G}^{t}_{D,X}\right)}+d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{G}^{t}_{D,X},\mathcal{G}_{D,X}\right)}.

We prove analogues of
Lemma

72
and
Lemma

73
.

Lemma 91
.

Assume the hypotheses of
Theorem

5
. Fix any
D^{\prime}\geq 3
. Suppose that
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}
is a
\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}
-approximate
k
-wise independent permutation of
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}
and
\mathcal{P}_{1}
is a
\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}
-approximate
k
-wise independent permutation of
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{1/D}}
. Then with the above definitions, for any
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}
,

\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}_{D^{\prime}}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}.

Lemma 92
.

Assume that
k\log k\leq n^{1/3}
, that
n
is large enough, and fix
D
. Then for all
t\geq 2500
, any
3\leq D^{\prime}\leq D
, and any
X\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}
,

\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}_{D^{\prime}}}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{D^{\prime}}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}.

We apply these two lemmas along with
Claim

70
to obtain the generalization of our main result to higher-dimensional lattices.

Proof of
Theorem

5
.

Fix
D
and set
t\geq 2500
as in
Lemma

92
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{1}
be a
\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}
-approximate
k
-wise independent permutation of
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{1/D}}
. Let
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}
be constructed from
\mathcal{P}_{1}
as in
Section

B.2
for all
2\leq D^{\prime}\leq D
.

We prove by induction on
D^{\prime}
that for all
D^{\prime}\leq D
, the random permutation
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}
is a
\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}
-approximate
k
-wise independent permutation of
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}
. In the base case
D^{\prime}=1
, this follows by assumption on
\mathcal{P}_{1}
. In the other base case
D^{\prime}=2
, this follows from
Theorem

71
.

Now fix
3\leq D^{\prime}\leq D
. Because
k\log k\leq n^{1/3}
so that the hypothesis of
Lemma

92
is satisfied. Assume that
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}
is a
\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}
-approximate
k
-wise independent permutation of
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}
. By
Lemma

91
and
Lemma

92
, we have that
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}^{t}
is a
\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}
-approximate
k
-wise independent permutation of
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}
:

\displaystyle d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{P}^{t}_{D^{\prime},X},\mathcal{G}_{X}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{P}^{t}_{D^{\prime},X},\mathcal{G}^{t}_{D^{\prime},X}\right)}+d_{\textrm{TV}}{\left(\mathcal{G}^{t}_{D^{\prime},X},\mathcal{G}_{D^{\prime},X}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}+\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}+\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}.

This completes the induction on
D^{\prime}
. As a result of the induction, we find that
\mathcal{P}_{D}
is a
\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}
-approximate
k
-wise independent permutation of
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D/D}}=\{\pm 1\}^{n}
.

To instantiate our construction, we take
\mathcal{P}_{1}
to be the depth
\widetilde{O}(k)\cdot(n^{1/D}k+n^{1/D}D\log n)=\widetilde{O}(n^{1/D}Dk^{2})
random one-dimensional brickwork circuit from
Theorem

3
. By
Theorem

2
, the random permutation
\mathcal{P}_{2}
is implemented by a random two-dimensional brickwork circuit of depth
\widetilde{O}(n^{1/D}Dk^{3})
. By the construction, if
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}
can be implemented by a random
D^{\prime}-1
-dimensional brickwork circuit of depth
\leq d
and
\mathcal{P}_{1}
can be implemented by a random one-dimensional brickwork of depth
\leq d
then
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}
can be implemented by a random
D^{\prime}
-dimensional brickwork circuit of depth
d\cdot(2t+1)
. This implies that
\mathcal{P}_{D}
can be implemented by a
D
-dimensional brickwork circuit of depth
(2t+1)^{D-2}\cdot\widetilde{O}(n^{1/D}Dk^{3})=\mathrm{exp}(D)\cdot\widetilde{O}(n^{1/D}k^{3})
.
∎

B.3.1
Proof of
Lemma

91

Following the proof of
Lemma

72
in the
D=2
case, we use
Fact

69
to bound:

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}_{D^{\prime}}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq(t+1)\cdot\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{P}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}+t\cdot\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{P}_{C}}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}.

To bound each of the two terms, we will establish the following two lemmas.

Lemma 93
.

Assume the hypothesis of
Lemma

91
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{P}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq n^{1/D}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}.

Lemma 94
.

Assume the hypothesis of
Lemma

91
. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{P}_{C}}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}.

Plugging directly into the equation above finishes the proof of
Lemma

91
.

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}_{D^{\prime}}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq(t+1)\cdot n^{1/D}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}+t\cdot n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}+\frac{1}{4}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}.

Note that we used the definitions of
\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}}^{t}
and
\mathcal{G}_{D^{\prime}}^{t}
from
Section

B.2
. This concludes the proof of
Lemma

91
.

Proof of
Lemma

93
.

Recall that
X,Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}
and we write
X_{i,\cdot}
for
i\in{\left[n^{1/D}\right]}
to denote one of
n^{1/D}

(D^{\prime}-1)
-dimensional slices. The operator
T_{\mathcal{P}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}}
can be seen as a
n^{1/D}
-wise tensorization of
T_{\mathcal{P}_{D-1}}
acting individually on each slice. As such, we compute:

\displaystyle\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{P}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{L,D^{\prime}-1}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{Y}{\left|\prod_{i=1}^{n^{1/D}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]-\prod_{i=1}^{n^{1/D}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{Y}{\left|\sum_{j=1}^{n^{1/D}}\prod_{i=1}^{j-1}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]{\left(\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}Y_{j,\cdot}]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}Y_{j,\cdot}]\right)}\prod_{i=j+1}^{n^{1/D}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{n^{1/D}}\sum_{Y}{\left|\prod_{i=1}^{j-1}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]{\left(\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}Y_{j,\cdot}]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}Y_{j,\cdot}]\right)}\prod_{i=j+1}^{n^{1/D}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{n^{1/D}}\sum_{y}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}y]\right|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}Y\\
Y_{j,\cdot}=y\end{subarray}}\prod_{i=1}^{j-1}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]\prod_{i=j+1}^{n^{1/D}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{i,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}Y_{i,\cdot}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{n^{1/D}}\sum_{y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}k}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X_{j,\cdot}\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}y]\right|}

\displaystyle\leq n^{1/D}\cdot\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}.

The last line follows from
Lemma

95
.
∎

Lemma 95
.

For every
x\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}k}
we have:

\sum_{y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}k}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}}y]\right|}\leq\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}.

Proof of
Lemma

95
.

We view
x
as a
k
-tuple of
(D^{\prime}-1)
-dimensional grids. We denote by
B
the “tuple-wise” color class of
x
(if two grids are equal they are colored the same). We create a projection function
\varphi_{B}
defined analogously to that in
Lemma

76
, taking
x
to a corresponding
\tau
-tuple with distinct elements.

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}k}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}}y]\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in B(x)}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[x\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}}y]\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in B(x)}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\varphi_{B}(x)\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}\varphi_{B}(y)]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\varphi_{B}(x)\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}}\varphi_{B}(y)]\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi_{B}(y)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}^{(\tau)}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\varphi_{B}(x)\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}\varphi_{B}(y)]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[\varphi_{B}(x)\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}}\varphi_{B}(y)]\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{\varphi_{B}(y)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}^{(\tau)}}{\left|\sum_{z\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}^{(k-\tau)}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[(\varphi_{B}(x),\cdot)\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),z)]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[(\varphi_{B}(x),\cdot)\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),z)]\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}\varphi_{B}(y)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}^{(\tau)}\\
z\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}^{(k-\tau)}\end{subarray}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[(\varphi_{B}(x),\cdot)\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),z)]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[(\varphi_{B}(x),\cdot)\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),z)]\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{y\in{\mathscr{D}}^{(k)}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}{\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[(\varphi_{B}(x),\cdot)\to_{T_{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),y_{[k]\setminus T})]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[(\varphi_{B}(x),\cdot)\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}}(\varphi_{B}(y),y_{[k]\setminus T})]\right|}.

The last step assumes
(\varphi_{B}(x),\cdot)\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}
, that is, it is a distinct
k
-tuple. We then appeal to the fact that
{\mathcal{P}_{D^{\prime}-1}}
is assumed to be
\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}
-approximate
k
-wise independent to finish.
∎

The proof of
Lemma

94
is nearly identical to that of
Lemma

93
, but partitioning
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}
over one-dimensional columns yields a tensor product of order
n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}
, which becomes a factor in the result, and additionally we appeal to the error in
\mathcal{P}_{1}
at the end.

B.3.2
Proof of
Lemma

92

This proof follows near identically to
Section

7.5
. Throughout this section we assume the hypothesis of
Lemma

92
, namely that
k\log k\leq n^{1/3}
. It suffices to prove for any
X\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}
via
Lemma

96
:

\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}_{D^{\prime}}}-T_{\mathcal{G}_{D^{\prime}}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{1}{(4(t+1)n)^{D-D^{\prime}+1}}\cdot\frac{1}{2^{n^{1/D}}}.

For clarity, we will assume all operators and distributions from this point on are implicitly parameterized by
D^{\prime}
and drop the subscript.

Lemma 96
.

Assume the hypotheses of
Lemma

92
. Then
{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{t+1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}(t-1)/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}
.

The lemma is used in the following calculation:

\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{t+1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}(t-1)/128}}\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}\leq\frac{k^{kn^{1/D}}\cdot(t+1)}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}(t-1)/128}}.

We use that the number of color classes is less than
k^{kn^{1/D}}
. Since
k\log k\leq n^{1/3}\leq n^{(D^{\prime}-2)/D}
for
D,D^{\prime}\geq 3
, we have that:

\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}\cdot(t+1)}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}(t-1)/128}}\leq\frac{1}{2^{(n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/128-1)(t-1)-1}}.

If we set
t=\frac{n^{1/D}D\log_{2}(4(t+1)n)+1}{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/128-1}+1
we achieve the desired bound. Note that for large enough
n
we have
t\leq\frac{2500D\log_{2}n}{n^{1/D}}
. Further if
D\leq\frac{1}{2}\cdot\frac{\log_{2}n}{\log_{2}\log_{2}n}
then we have
n^{1/D}\geq(\log_{2}n)^{2}
, which is enough to conclude
t\leq 2500
. This concludes the proof of
Lemma

92
.

Proof of
Lemma

96
.

Recall
T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}=T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}})^{t}
so
T_{\mathcal{G}^{t}}-T_{\mathcal{G}}=(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})
. We induct on
t
. Consider first when
t=0
.

{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}={\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}}Y]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}}}Y]\right|}={\left|\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[Y\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}}X]-\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[Y\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}}}X]\right|}.

Note that we guarantee inductively that
\mathcal{G}_{L}
is self-adjoint. This quantity is bounded by
\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}
as before.

For the induction step, assume the lemma for
t\geq 0
and compute

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t+1}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}=

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left|T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}{\left(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}\right)}(X)\right|}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\left|\sum_{Z\in{\mathscr{D}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}}Z]\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\sum_{Z\in B_{\text{safe}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}}Z]\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;+{\left|\sum_{Z\in B_{\text{coll}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}}Z]\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{Z\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;+\frac{1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/128}}\cdot\max_{Z\in B_{\mathrm{coll}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{Z\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}{\left|\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}+\frac{t+1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}t/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}.

We apply the induction in the last line and
Lemma

98
as stated below in the previous line, in order to bound the probability
X
lands in the collision region.

Lemma 97
.

Assume the hypotheses of
Lemma

92
. Then
\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}}\right\rVert}_{2}\leq\frac{1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/128}}
.

Lemma 98
.

Assume the hypotheses of
Lemma

92
. Then for all
X\in{\mathscr{D}}
,
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}}B_{\text{coll}}]\leq\frac{1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/128}}
.

We prove these two lemmas in
Section

B.4
. The use of
Lemma

98
is in bounding the latter term above. To use
Lemma

97
we write for
Z\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}
:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}=

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}e_{Z}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})^{t}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}}\right\rVert}_{2}^{t}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}e_{Z}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}e_{Y}\right\rVert}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}t/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}^{1/2}{\left|B_{\text{safe}}\right|}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}t/128}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B(Y)\right|}}.

The first step uses the self-adjointness of
T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}
, the fact that
T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}^{2}=T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}
, and
Fact

68
. The inequality is an application of Cauchy-Schwarz and submultiplicativity of the operator norm. The second to last step uses
Lemma

97
and
Claim

99
below, and the last step uses
Fact

90
, namely that
B_{\mathrm{safe}}
is larger than every other color class for our choice of
k
and large enough
n
.

Claim 99
.

For arbitrary
U\in\{\pm 1\}^{nk}
:

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}e_{U}\right\rVert}_{2}=\frac{1}{{\left|B(U)\right|}^{1/2}}.

Proof.

Observe:

\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}e_{U}\right\rVert}_{2}=\sqrt{\sum_{W\in B(U)}{\left(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}e_{U}(W)\right)}^{2}}=\sqrt{\sum_{W\in B(U)}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[W\to_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}U]^{2}}=\sqrt{\sum_{W\in B(U)}{\left(\frac{1}{{\left|B(U)\right|}}\right)}^{2}}=\frac{1}{{\left|B(U)\right|}^{1/2}}.\qed

Putting the two together then gives us:

{\left|\left\langle{{e_{X}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}})^{t+1}(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})e_{Y}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}t/128}}+\frac{t+1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}t/128}}\leq\frac{t+2}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}t/128}}.

This concludes the proof of
Lemma

96
.
∎

B.4
Proof of Spectral Properties

In this section we prove
Lemma

97
and
Lemma

98
. We will proceed by decomposing
f=f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}+f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}+f_{B_{=0}}
where
f_{B}
is supported on
B\subseteq\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}
.

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\;\;+{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{=0}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{B_{=0}}}}\right\rangle\right|}.

B.4.1
f
Supported on
B_{\mathrm{safe}}

Lemma 100
.

{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{4n^{1/D}k^{2}}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}\cdot\left\langle{{f},{f}}\right\rangle
.

Proof.

Let
X\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}
,
g\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}\to\mathbb{R}
.

\displaystyle(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})g(X)

\displaystyle=\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}}Y]\cdot g(Y)-\sum_{Y\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}}}Y]\cdot g(Y)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{{\left|B_{\mathrm{safe}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}g(Y)-\frac{1}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}g(Y)

\displaystyle={\left(\frac{1}{{\left|B_{\mathrm{safe}}\right|}}-\frac{1}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}\right)}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}g(Y)-\frac{1}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{coll}}}g(Y)

\displaystyle={\left(1-\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{safe}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}\right)}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B_{\mathrm{safe}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}g(Y)-\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}\cdot\frac{1}{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{coll}}}g(Y)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}{\left(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-\mathcal{H}\right)}g(X),

where
\mathcal{H}g(X)=\frac{1}{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}\sum_{Y\in B_{\mathrm{coll}}}g(Y)
. We write:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle=\sum_{X\in\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}}f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}(X)\cdot(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}})(X)

\displaystyle=\sum_{X\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}(X)\cdot(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}})(X)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}\sum_{X\in B_{\mathrm{safe}}}f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}(X)\cdot(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-\mathcal{H})(T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}})(X)

\displaystyle=\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-\mathcal{H}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}.

The fact that
\mathcal{H}
is a random walk operator once again establishes:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}{\left({\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}+{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}},{\mathcal{H}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}\right\rVert}_{2}\mathinner{\!\left\lVert f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}\right\rVert}_{2}

(
Lemma

23
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}\langle f,f\rangle.

Fact

90
then suffices to prove the claim. ∎

B.4.2
f
Supported on
B_{\mathrm{coll}}

Lemma 101
.

{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\frac{8n^{1/D}k^{2}}{2^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}/32}}\left\langle{{f},{f}}\right\rangle
.

Proof.

First, we can decompose
f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}=f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}+f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}
and bound:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{B_{\mathrm{safe}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle+{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}.

By the self-adjointness of the operator, the first term is bounded by the case above, so it suffices to bound the latter. For this term, we can appeal directly to
Lemma

23
and the triangle inequality to get:

\displaystyle{\left|\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}-T_{\mathcal{G}})f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}}}\right\rangle\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\max_{X\in B_{\mathrm{coll}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}}B_{\mathrm{coll}}]+\max_{X\in B_{\mathrm{coll}}}\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}}}B_{\mathrm{coll}}]}\left\langle{{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}},{f_{B_{\mathrm{coll}}}}}\right\rangle.

Note that regardless of choice of
X
, the latter probability
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}}}B_{\mathrm{coll}}]=\frac{{\left|B_{\mathrm{coll}}\right|}}{{\left|{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}\right|}}\leq\frac{2n^{1/D}k^{2}}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}
by
Fact

90
. We finish by proving
Lemma

98
from the previous section below.
∎

Lemma 102

(Restatement of
Lemma

98
)
.

For all
X\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}
,
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}}B_{\mathrm{coll}}]\leq\frac{2n^{1/D}k^{2}}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/16}}
.

Proof.

Our goal is to union bound over the probability of any pair of sublattices colliding. There are at most
n^{1/D}k^{2}
pairs of sublattices.

Let
X\in{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}
. We will model our process as:

X\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}}Y\to_{T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}}Z.

We then fix
Z^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}
and
Z^{m}_{i,\cdot}
(which recall are
(D^{\prime}-1)
-dimensional slices, in
\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}
) for
i\in[n^{1/D}]
,
\ell\neq m\in[k]
. We use that for some
j\in[k]
, we have
(Y^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},Y^{m}_{j,\cdot})
are uniform from
{\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}\choose 2}
. To see this note that there must exist some
j
s.t.
X^{\ell}_{j,\cdot}\neq X^{m}_{j,\cdot}
, otherwise
X\notin{\mathscr{D}}_{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}
. Since the permutation applied to these two grids is uniform from
\mathfrak{S}{\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}
, the resulting rows in
Y
look like a uniform distinct pair.

With this in mind, we will now condition on the event that
d(Y^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},Y^{m}_{j,\cdot})\geq n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4
and compute for
n
large enough that

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[Z^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}=Z^{m}_{i,\cdot}]=

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[Z^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}=Z^{m}_{i,\cdot}\mid d(Y^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},Y^{m}_{j,\cdot})>n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[Z^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}=Z^{m}_{i,\cdot}\mid d(Y^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},Y^{m}_{j,\cdot})\leq n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4]\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[d(Y^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},Y^{m}_{j,\cdot})\leq n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[Z^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}=Z^{m}_{i,\cdot}\mid d(Y^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},Y^{m}_{j,\cdot})>n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4]+\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[d(Y^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},Y^{m}_{j,\cdot})\leq n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/16}}+\frac{1}{e^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/16}}

(
Lemma

103
,
Lemma

104
)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/32}}.

Applying a union bound over all
n^{1/D}k^{2}
pairs of sublattices completes the proof.
∎

Lemma 103
.

\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[Z^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}=Z^{m}_{i,\cdot}\mid d(Y^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},Y^{m}_{j,\cdot})>n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4]\leq\frac{1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4}}

Proof.

The probability that
Z^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}
and
Z^{m}_{i,\cdot}
are equal can be viewed as the probability that all of their individual bits are equal, and they are all independent since they come from independently sampled rows. Since
Y^{\ell}_{j,\cdot}
and
Y^{m}_{j,\cdot}
differ in at least
n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4
places,
Y^{\ell}
and
Y^{m}
must differ in at least that many rows. In these rows, it can be seen that the corresponding bits in
Z^{\ell}_{i,\cdot}
and
Z^{m}_{i,\cdot}
are the same with probability
\leq\frac{1}{2}
. By independence the probability is less than
\frac{1}{2^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4}}
.
∎

Lemma 104
.

\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[d(Y^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},Y^{m}_{j,\cdot})\leq n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4]\leq\frac{1}{e^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/16}}

Proof.

This can be seen by a simple Chernoff bound. Note that
\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}[d(Y^{\ell}_{j,\cdot},Y^{m}_{j,\cdot})\leq n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4]\leq\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{x,y\sim\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}}}[d(x,y)\leq n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4]
, as if they are equal the distance is minimized. For uniform
x,y
,
d(x,y)
can be seen as the sum of
n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}
independent Bernoulli
(1/2)
r.v.s. By Hoeffding’s Inequality:

\operatorname{{\bf Pr}}_{x,y\sim\{\pm 1\}^{n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}}}[d(x,y)\leq n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/4]\leq e^{-n^{(D^{\prime}-1)/D}/16}.\qed

B.4.3
The Induction Case

Lemma 105
.

Let
f\mathrel{\mathop{\ordinarycolon}}\{\pm 1\}^{n^{D^{\prime}/D}k}\to\mathbb{R}
be supported on
B_{=0}
and
k\geq 2
. Then, we have

{\left|\left\langle{{f},{{\left(T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}^{(k)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}^{(k)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}^{(k)}-T_{\mathcal{G}}^{(k)}\right)}f}}\right\rangle\right|}\leq\mathinner{\!\left\lVert T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}^{(k-1)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{C}}^{(k-1)}T_{\mathcal{G}_{L}}^{(k-1)}-T_{\mathcal{G}}^{(k-1)}\right\rVert}_{2}\left\langle{{f},{f}}\right\rangle.

Proof.

The proof is nearly notationally identical to
Lemma

40
as the notion of color class developed in that section is on the tuple so is not dependent on the choice of sublattice, so we will refer back for brevity.
∎
